CAP.  XXX.

The tincture, which we speak of, son, has been extracted from our gold, and we ferment it in common gold, which carries it from potency to act, nor in this

--- Page 138 (Theorica Cap. XXX cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
were we entirely certain, nor could we believe, until we had seen the power of our magnesia, which by its great heat congeals the spirit through the transformation of its exuberant nature, multiplied in permanent heat. And this fermentation has been made with the aid of fixed red sulfur, and afterwards it is nothing but fire, water, and earth, full of the fifth essence, and through water the earth is washed, through fire the earth is tinted and colored, and then it is manifest to every man. For this is the tincture, which strips itself of vile earth, and clothes itself again in another much more noble one. And know why our first venom does not make a gilded fume, when Mercury is sublimated from it. It is because in its belly Mercury has received the whole tincture of the fume, and it is impregnated by the said fume, which is our said fire, and through successive coction, which in its nature corresponds to the nature of the said fire, which is hidden in the belly of the said congealed quicksilver — through the extension of the subtle parts of its essential igneity, in directing the parts of the said Mercury, it colors and transmutes it into its own proper nature of fire, which is pure tincture, fixed in the material essence of individual

--- Page 139 (page 61, Theorica Cap. XXX cont. / Cap. XXXI) ---
THEORICA.
metals, which we call the Mercurial spirit, since in it the spirit is manifestly dissolved, and is still capable of dissolving within the same, and will be so as long as it can, because thus nature is turned through circulation.

On the method of proceeding in practice,
and how much is required
in the magisterium.

CAP.  XXXI.

To instruct the son of the art concerning this matter, great knowledge is needed, namely, revelation, or demonstrable teaching, so that he may enter the practice of our magisterium, and he requires a strong and great theory, which reveals the strengths through the will of nature. And if you are negligent in reading through and scrutinizing the content of our books, you will go blind to the practice like an ignorant man. And therefore, son, you must premeditate upon what matter you should set your hand to work. And you must know how many and what things are principally required in our magisterium, or in the proximity of its operation. And therefore, son, I tell you that three things are required, namely: a subtle, natural understanding, not sophistical; manual operation; and free judgment — and this requires wisdom, wealth,

--- Page 140 (Theorica Cap. XXXI cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
and books. Wisdom, in order to know how to do. Wealth, in order to have the power of doing. Books, in order to open the diverse understanding that exists among many peoples. For that which one person comprehends through the teaching of one, can never be comprehended except through the teaching of another. And thus the teaching of one will open the teaching of another, perhaps given through figure and according to the tradition of greater or lesser authorities. But here we shall manifest to you in a clear voice the principal hidden thing, for entering the secrets of the secrets of nature. Whence you will see many beautiful wonders, which are stored away in the secrets of nature, and will be considered almost as miracles through the secret of Lady Nature. In this it is necessary that you begin to enter in the following manner. Son, it is necessary for you first to have in your knowledge the formative virtue, recalled from what has been said above, which depends on our magisterium through a certain known science. Because the art, that is, the science, when it is truly known, without any ignorance, through the sense of the understanding, which comes from the noble operator, informs the matter to that being, to which the natural instrumental heat,

--- Page 141 (page 62, Theorica Cap. XXXI cont.) ---
THEORICA.
in which the active virtue is infused by the informative virtue, is directed in its matter to operate and form directly: then it appears that the said instrumental heat is directed through the formative virtue, and the formative virtue depends on the knowledge of the operator, in guarding the course of nature in the magisterium, through which it is infused into the matter, because the excited and increased heat itself moves it. Just as in the nature of every other thing the formative virtue is infused from the heaven and from the stars. Because heaven is the moving principle of all nature, just as the wise operator is in our magisterium, through the teaching of his noble understanding. Let it therefore be revealed to you that two things are necessary in our magisterium, namely, the informative virtue and the elevated instrument through which our magisterium must be formed. The informative virtue is governed by four principal virtues, convertible and converting, namely, heat, cold, moisture, and dryness, to the end that the matter may be treated with a directed instrument and conducted and brought to that act through which it must undergo its operation

--- Page 142 (Theorica Cap. XXXI cont. / Cap. XXXII) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
according to the intention of the converting and convertible, and in the action the matter must be informed, so that through the information itself the instrument may be governed to the completion of its operation, corresponding to the will of the operator in the matter informed through art according to the requirement of venerable nature, which is sagacious and intent upon arriving at and perfecting its work of perfection.

On the four natural mutative virtues,
and how they are
called, and how
they operate.

CAP.  XXXII.

Understand, son, that since the said formative virtue must govern itself through the four aforesaid elemental qualities, from which without intermediary and successively the four natural mutative virtues are caused. And therefore you must divide gradually the information which every good operator must infuse into suitable matter, and successively into four parts, namely: into the attractive nature, and through this is caused the congelation of the spirit and the dissolution of the

--- Page 143 (page 63, Theorica Cap. XXXII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
body; into the retentive and coagulative nature; and into the expulsive nature; and the contentive and digestive nature. The attractive nature operates through heat and tempered dryness; the retentive nature through dryness and tempered coldness; the expulsive through moisture and tempered heat; and the digestive nature through heat and tempered moisture. And in that action in which the matter operates best in its complexion, you must assist it, because according to the greater or lesser strength of its complexion, the matter will show you its speed or slowness, or the temperament of the difference of its transmutation, whence its secondary effects are caused, which are drawn from potency into act, such as heaviness, lightness, gravity, hardness, softness, sweetness, bitterness, whiteness, and blackness, etc. Know therefore, son, to what virtue, to what form you must direct your matter: whether to the appetitive virtue, or to the digestive virtue, or to the retentive virtue, or to the expulsive virtue. Because it is necessary for you that the matter receive such information through known and understood art, in such a form that its instrument be moved there and well di-

--- Page 144 (Theorica Cap. XXXII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
rected, up to the end of its operation, which end shows a certain sign to its magisterium. Wherefore if he shall have in his memory the signs, he will have certain knowledge and the ability to correct and amend the strengthening and weakening, which are the extremities of natural operations. And therefore understand that fire is the instrument of nature, just as the instrument by which the smith works is the hammer, which moves the matter of iron to such a disposition as is the intention of the smith, who informs the hammer through formal motion. Thus indeed does the heat of nature in our magisterium, because according to art it is directed to the intention of the Artist. For it is directed either to destroying and corrupting, or to generating, or to binding, or otherwise. And therefore it is necessary for you that through this known science you have the direction of choosing and knowing such a fire, according to which, formed through art, the matter must be changed from nature into nature, until the motion of the supreme nature, which is in the fire, through the termination and expulsion of extraneous motions in its own proper matter, without any corruption, can perpetually rest. And this is the heat of all generated things, just as the heat of the sun is the effect

--- Page 145 (page 64, Theorica Cap. XXXII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
the principal cause of all generation. Therefore let it be certain to you that if the art of the operator is uncertain in disposing the matter, our stone cannot be formed in it in potency, in which formally and naturally the entire course of virtues that are in the center of our medicine is enclosed. Blessed be God, glorious and omnipotent, who gave to the human race the understanding of administering, adding, and integrating the confused particularities of all medicine into a real universality, through which they shall remain entirely rectified. And therefore, son, we admonish you, if you desire to be a perfect physician, do not make yourself complacent with the particularities of medicine, because they are confused, nor are they whole today. For nature cannot endure them on account of their excessive confusion, but take delight in a single medicine, because there is only one that is curative of all malice, depravity, and infirmity, and fortifying of spiritual virtues. Therefore hold and maintain the secure opinion of the hemorrhoidal [?]. For the whole science of medicine is, and can be, stably reduced to the opinion of those who only contemplate in universality, in which is the congregation of operative virtues — understand this by the course of nature. For he who knows how to reduce many and more particularities to universality

--- Page 146 (Theorica Cap. XXXII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
shall be better among all others. For in the particularities are confused virtues, and in the universalities are real virtues congregated and excited into one, just as the course of all nature manifests, and this is the medicine of medicines. And he who has such a medicine has a gift of God, because it is an incomparable treasure. Therefore recover the science through which you may inform nature, from which such a jewel and such a good is created and formed by artifice, surpassing the entire course of nature. For if the art operates upon uncertain matter, it cannot be well disposed according to the requirement of nature's intention, nor can nature create that which remains to it, nor that which the operator seeks, because the matter has failed in its formative virtue. And because the instrument which was designed for motion was not rightly directed, and this was caused entirely by ignorance in the understanding, when the Artist does not have the science of knowing the certain information from which the said matter must receive its motion, through the influence of science known by the natural operator. And therefore when ignorance and uncertainty of science flows in, nature deviates from the understanding of such an artificer.

--- Page 147 (page 65, Theorica Cap. XXXII cont. / Cap. XXXIII) ---
THEORICA.
And also you must well consider that the virtue which is in the medicine should not be united to it more by extraneous heat than it can direct. For the burning and combustive heat that comes from elemental fire has been given to it by an instrument, which thing was never the intention of nature, and therefore it failed to form that which the Artist sought.

On the information of natural heat
through excitation made by common
fire, or on the information of
the natural with heat
that is against
nature.

CAP. XXXIII.

Son, you can understand that natural fire alone, or the fire of nature strengthened with non-natural fire, with the operation of strengthening, or creation, or transmutation, operates uniformly in matter excited solely by fire against nature, in such a way that the natural fire, in which is the active virtue, is not suppressed by elemental fire. And in

--- Page 148 (Theorica Cap. XXXIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
the first operation of its corruption you must observe that the fire against nature should surpass the natural innate heat in the noble subject by only one degree, and not beyond that number. For the individual spirit, which is preservative of the species, desiring a like generation by the right instinct of its nature, would be corrupted through the destruction of its essence, and then it would no longer have the appetite for producing a like generation. For nature would depart through corruption and destruction, which are against nature, and would receive its termination and the end of its conversion, because it would have no place in which to rest, nor air in which to breathe. And the emptiness must enter, of which the place was full of extraneous nature. And you must have a large alembic, so that the matter may ascend more freely, and be excited with a small fire, so that it may come into air. And it is not necessary that through excited fire the air be much condensed into water, because the active virtue would be submerged and suffocated too much in the water, and the natural tinctures would be burned, when the action of the heat of elemental fire would occupy the parts of natural fire, or the place where it is carried.

--- Page 149 (page 66, Theorica Cap. XXXIII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
Just as it is seen that a great flame destroys a small one and converts it into itself. For we do not wish that the fire against nature should convert the natural fire in its entire essential and accidental substance, but rather that the natural fire should convert by its own first power the fire against nature, in the part already altered. And therefore let it be revealed to you that two certain operations are necessary for you, namely, corruption, which is made under the preservation of its form, and through fire against nature with the aid of the excitation of elemental fire and the neutrality of non-natural fire. The other operation is generation, which is done with natural fire multiplied by non-natural fire and fire against nature. For these two fires are converted into the proper natural fire, namely, the non-natural fire of itself and per accidens, and the fire against nature per accidens through the attractive love of non-natural fire. And in this natural fire they are found and converted. Wherefore, therefore, they are between themselves both instruments of nature, hot and much tempered, of very strong virtue, which is high and most powerful for making

--- Page 150 (Theorica Cap. XXXIII cont. / Cap. XXXIIII) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
a new regeneration or a binding together of many virtues conjoined in one proper subject, which is the secret of nature joined by the art of the magisterium to the nature of the radical moisture.

How the matter of our quicksilver
is found. And it can be dis-
covered in every elemen-
tal body.

CAP. XXXIIII.

If you wish, son, to have the effect of our magisterium, you must not be ignorant of the nature of our quicksilver, which is found in every elemental body. And every elemental body, in whatever form nature has formed it, is composed of the four principal elements, as is manifestly evident through the revolution of the circle of nature, which shows that no thing created or generated by nature shall now be of so diverse a form or figure that we may not find through natural principles that it is composed of one material genus, namely, quicksilver, which indeed we wish to name the nearer general genus of nature, as

--- Page 151 (page 67, Theorica Cap. XXXIIII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
its own proper natural subject, in which lady nature paints and draws forth her diverse figures. And note, when we say "nearer," we say it to distinguish it from the universal genus, from which this one descends. And when we say "general," we say it to distinguish it from the greater nearness of another genus that it has with nature. And therefore since it is of this genus, it is agreed upon, when through art or nature it is appropriated to the first form of the universal genus, from which all other genera descend, which are our quicksilvers. And therefore, since all genera come by natural gradual succession from the first quicksilver, which is the most general, remote from the principal and nearer, the medium of nature — wherefore it has been called the first matter to every succeeding genus — nor do they thus discord or differ in the nature of the first genus, which is called universal or most general in our philosophy, whence the logicians and sophists have been deceived, because they only grasp it in species or in figure, as you manifestly see every day, that the genus of nourishment is converted into the genus of the nourished thing, which nature could not do unless the genus of the converting thing had a concordance with the genus and nature of the con-

--- Page 152 (Theorica Cap. XXXIIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
verted thing. This concordance, son, descends from the first rib or side of the genus into three diverse genera, namely, animal, vegetable, and mineral. From the first genus comes vapor, generated from the elements immediately and without a medium. And this vapor is of the first composition, and from the second are mineral bodies, and from the third composition are vegetables. From the fourth come beasts and brutes, and from the fifth are humans. But all these descend from the most principal of principal genera, from which nature receives the nature of quicksilver, to create every other composite. And every composite, by reason of the said matter, is transmuted as in its first Mercurial nature into another, as has been said, into the nature and matter of another composite — namely, that the vegetable Mercurial genus is transmuted into the animal and mineral genus, and the animal genus into the mineral — that is, when it is appropriated and conjoined by retrogradation to the second degree of composition, in which are mineral bodies. Thus nature makes such a transmutation by a subtle separation of the pure from the impure, namely, of the homogeneous thing from the heterogeneous. For the intention of nature is such that when it wishes to dispose toward the destruction

--- Page 153 (page 68, Theorica Cap. XXXIV cont.) ---
THEORICA.
its composition, immediately thinks to separate the nature that participates with the fifth thing, and in that it saves and preserves the first species, because it can receive no other counsel. And if the matter is so agitated and given over to corruption that the preservation of the species cannot be accomplished, and grosser matter remains, then nature transmutes the said matter into the genus of another composite — vegetable, animal, or mineral. For according to how far the corruption extends in potency, to that degree the matter is corrupted. And thus it is transmuted into that or another or a similar thing, so that if the simple matter of the fifth composition is corrupted without preservation of its form, it immediately seeks the matter of the fourth composition, through nature that is immediately gross. But this division cannot be accomplished by art, nor by nature toward the perfection of nature, unless the converting agent renders the whole matter homogeneous, and the part of it be similar to the totality, or the form be simple, as is the form of the elements, or a mixed form, as is the form of stone or of metals. And this homogeneity has been made so that the matter may be separated from all forms that are not of the sequence of the harmonious mixture of the bod-

--- Page 154 (page 68 verso, Theorica Cap. XXXIV cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
ies toward which nature or art intends to constitute the said matter. Or perhaps the said converting agent is natural or against nature. If it is natural, then fusible stones will be created through heat and dryness. And if against nature, then non-fusible stones will be created, in which there remained no moisture that was to be informed by the converting nature, except according to the continuity of the matter. For this reason stones cannot be melted, nor are they melted. And when the said matter has been dispersed through degrees of separation, then it is near to the first cause. And the nearer it is to that, the more it can be multiplied in the most noble virtues — namely, when the body from which perfection comes, which we call the fixed unguent, in which and where it has its tinctures and figures, which are the multiplied tinctures of goldness or silverness, through which figures the virtue of the most noble matter accomplishes its effects and offices, and the proper actions of its transmutation, just as we have seen in its projection upon both mineral and animal bodies.

On the supreme genus from which all nature proceeded.

--- Page 155 (page 69, Theorica Cap. XXXV) ---
THEORICA.
CAP. XXXV.

Therefore, son, you ought to be sufficiently enlightened, and to see that unless there were a universal and supreme genus by which nature is governed, no thing that was to be created or generated by nature could exist, nor could any animate thing live without the sustenance of the supreme genus, which genus is the matter of nature — but nature would lose its being. And since such a genus exists in material being, although it is confusedly and potentially in the substance and matter of every genus, it is necessary for you that the transmutation of its form, through certain degrees of separation, should convert the said matters from potency to action, namely, into prime matter — not however remote from the metallic genus, but proximate and near to the line or to the genus in which they were at their beginning. From which matter or genus nature began to work before any form was introduced into prime matter, or generated in the nearer or more proximate genus.

On the matter that desires to have unity of form, so that from its perfec-

--- Page 156 (page 69 verso, Theorica Cap. XXXVI) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
tion it may be made whole in the unity of the spirit of the fifth essence.

CAP. XXXVI.

This prime matter, son, is a subtle sulfurous earth, which greatly desires integration from the unity of the spirit of the fifth essence, just as any proper matter desires some proper form, for the cause and reason of its completion. And therefore let the matter of living quicksilver be prepared through seven distillations, so that the matter may be pure, with which through resolution a pure form may be obtained. And therefore let this be understood in dry and moist matter. And therefore the nobler and better the matter is, the more it desires a nobler form, and the nobler the form, the more it ennobles its matter. And after the matter has been made fit to resume that form — as it is when it has been appropriated either by art or by nature to the act of the first thing — then it is very fit to receive the mineral golden form, which clari-

--- Page 157 (page 70, Theorica Cap. XXXVI cont.) ---
THEORICA.
fies, illuminates, and thoroughly ennobles it. Therefore this noble earth, it has been said, is the Mercurial subject, since it has been returned to the first substance of living quicksilver, and in it receives its termination and end. And in this earth, son, you must fix the elements, in which it is necessary that the fume of our gold come to rest as at the first element, which is the subject and foundation of all the others that are to be fixed by it. Therefore, because it is the most material of all the others, although it is very subtle and spiritual in the form of white earth of great and mighty virtue. If this, son, is hidden from you, you must be ignorant of our entire art and science, because it is the principal foundation of our whole magisterium. And if you wish to have knowledge of it, through our doctrine it is and shall be revealed to you. In every place you will find it, and from which you shall draw it, as we shall say in the second part, because it is of sulfur and quicksilver, as appears from its property, when it receives in itself the red and sanguine tincture of the supreme metal, and takes on perpetual ingress and fusion — not however without our magisterium revealed to you, son, in the second part. And because the property of quicksilver is to dissolve and whit-

--- Page 158 (page 70 verso, Theorica Cap. XXXVI cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
en its sulfur, and the property of sulfur is to congeal and redden its Mercury, you will find it furnished with such virtues received from the first genus. And it still has many other properties of perfection, as you will hear afterwards. Know however that it is not common quicksilver in its whole substance, nor in its nature, but it is philosophical quicksilver, namely of gold and silver, brought near to the genus created from real beings, by certain measures of nature, through the art already known.

How all things in the world are of sulfur and of quicksilver, as of a universal matter, and of their successors, and that it is found in every place through its properties.

CAP. XXXVII.

Furthermore, son, we make known to you that every thing in the world composed of the substance of the four elements substantially is nothing but sulfur and quicksilver —

--- Page 159 (page 71, Theorica Cap. XXXVII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
pure, clean, and incombustible at the first point of their creation. And this you can know and see through the art, which does this through the operation of our magisterium — namely, when the moist, earthy, dry substance is congealed, just as appears through wine, from which the Mercurial substance is converted into human nature. This is the first germ, or first matter, and the middle substance, in which nature plants and fixes all its colors, forming diverse substances. These colors come from the property of the nature of the conjoined sulfur and quicksilver through their instinct and excitation. Furthermore, this prime matter has many other properties, through which we know that it is of the nature of sulfur and quicksilver. For it cannot be corrupted in fire, nor in air, water, or earth. And it has the properties of dissolving, congealing, drawing into appetite, attracting, retaining, expelling, driving out, and digesting — which is the perfection and completion of the animate thing in its contrary passions. Through these properties it is most certainly established that it is of the nature of sulfur and quicksilver — just as in performing contrary operations, which it could not perform unless it were of the nature and property of both.

--- Page 160 (page 71 verso, Theorica Cap. XXXVII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
And since the said properties cannot exist without substance, just as no accident can exist without matter, we say that this substance is nothing but sulfur and quicksilver, collected according to their composition. Through which it is clear that our stone is found in every place by reason of its properties, and from that experience we know that it is the prime matter of all universal things, which have been formed through generation and corruption according to their composition. Wherefore it has been declared to us that it is of the nature of sulfur and quicksilver, and on account of this it is not quicksilver in the whole of its substance, nor in the whole of its nature, with respect to common quicksilver, but it is in part of it. And therefore it amicably adheres to its own nature, and is perfective of it, by reason of its powerful digestion, through which the raw is converted into the cooked. For then nature rejoices in nature, and is its mother and the dwelling-place of its nature through the concordance of that which is moved by the first cause. And therefore it congeals the remainder, and they fix themselves perpetually and join without any disjunction — which thing could not happen unless the nature of one were participating by affinity in the nature of the other. And this

--- Page 161 (page 72, Theorica Cap. XXXVII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
this nearness and proximity of the first genus, from which all things proceeded. And therefore we say it is manifestly known that this prime matter is of the nature of quicksilver, and is near to it, since in its projection it defends common quicksilver against fire from combustion without flight, and congeals it, and wonderfully retains it after its perfect fixation. Whence it should be noted that the nature of the medicine is nothing other than quicksilver well digested and cooked by the nature of its purified sulfur. And since the matter of quicksilver is of the nature of metal, therefore it penetrates it and mixes with it, because it is of the substance and nature of that metal. Wherefore you should understand that quicksilver must first be fixed in a certain decoction of moisture, descending from a certain property, which is the digestion and perfection of it through the ferment of the sun and the moon, before you make projection upon common quicksilver, because it is entirely aerial and would fly completely from the fire, but after fermentation it would remain, because its entire substance is incombustible and aerial, which thing is operative and a likeness of all perfection.

--- Page 162 (Theorica Cap. XXXVIII) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
How our stone is found in every place. And concerning the ingenuity by which it may be found.
CAP. XXXVIII.
Since therefore, son, we have told you that from such materials all the things of the world receive their substantial, accidental, and spiritual form, on account of this we say that you will find our stone in every place, since from it all the things of the world were created. This being known, see with what material you wish to become a philosopher, because from every thing, or some of them, you will extract the prime matter, even from potency into act, in deed and actually, as we have already revealed to you. And that from its matter alone, in good truth, magnitude, and power, they will have been created. For example, when a smith wishes to make a nail, it is necessary that he take suitable material, namely iron, because without the material of iron he cannot fashion a nail; then through his instruments he must shape it, by the design coming from his intellect, until he arrives at the desired end. So it is with nature, because when it wishes to begin to shape some

--- Page 163 (page 73, Theorica Cap. XXXVIII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
thing in substantial form, it takes the simple prime matter, which is without complete form, and in it begins to fix the elements, which are of diverse nature, according as they are purified and made more simple in their natural preparation by the action of tempered heat, shaping diverse forms, colors, odors, flavors, and in diverse complexions, according to the diversity of the mixtures of elements in this prime matter, whether it be in animals, or vegetables, or mineral things, because it has a convertible regard toward all things with respect to the said matter by reason of its incompleteness. Therefore it can be converted into all things, as it were a universal seed of all things, according to the operation of the influences of things and of conjoined causes, and according to the attraction of the place in which the matter exists. And on account of this, the genus similar to the first, in which the matter exists through the individuality of the first genus, which is called the most general genus, which through natural corruption with art is drawn from that matter already completely formed by nature and through generation, we convert and congeal into its own nature similar to the form of its first

--- Page 164 (Theorica Cap. XXXVIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
genus, which is called quicksilver, which is the subject receptive of all colors and forms, with the conjunction of its elements, such as are appropriated to it, for the property of matter is to desire infinite forms. Therefore a noble form must be joined to this noble matter. Let it therefore have the noblest elements, which contain the form or species that you seek, because it will receive such things as you will minister to it, and from them it will be tinged, and will be entirely converted. Because the property of ferment is to turn the stone into the nature of the ferment, namely into a nature that is liquefiable and flowing by reason of a subtle oiliness, and of an incombustible and fermentable oil, which gently enters all parts of the said matter, which is submerged in a permanent liquor, which is afterward called the powerful ferment. For in its nature it converts every metal, according to the power limited to it, and according as its elements are pure and tempered. So likewise it will be tempered. And since this matter is converted into every form and into every tempered complexion to which it is appropriated, we call it our Mercury, after the likeness of the celestial planet Mercury, which is found to be cold, hot, moist,

--- Page 165 (page 74, Theorica Cap. XXXVIII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
and dry according to the revolution of the circle, or is tempered or untempered according to the diversity of more or less of its oppositions, conjunctions, or aspects. And in that manner this stone, when it is in water, then it is very cold, and when it is in fire, then it is very hot, and when it is in air, then it is very moist, and so with the other elements. And this temperament must be chosen as Arnoldus de Villanova says in his Rosarium in chapter 14, which begins, "All things under a defined limit," etc., and at the end, etc. Likewise, son, this matter converts itself to the nature with which it is joined, both jointly and separately, according to the mode and virtue of the conjunction or division of the earth of ferment. Therefore, son, if you wish that it be turned to the nature of metal, join it with the nature of the metal with which it is brought near, up to the point of its virtuous fermentation, namely, that the dry, which is the matter of metallic sulfur, be joined with the matter of the moist of metal, namely air, so that it may flow and melt, because from man comes man, from bull comes bull, and from metal comes metal. And in this you will avoid the errors of those who in their time made a stone that cooks and is cooked without effec-

--- Page 166 (Theorica Cap. XXXVIII cont. / Cap. XXXIX) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
tive fusion, which does not come from the property of metal, which is liquefiable and flowing. And you should note that there is no metallic ferment except in gold and silver, because they are the tinctures by which our quicksilver is colored. And whoever believes he can tinge metals without such quicksilver goes blind to the practice and to the effect of his transmutations, for gold tinges metals in an aureate red color, like blood, and silver tinges metals in a color of whiteness, well penetrating and entering every metallic body. If therefore you know how to tinge with gold and silver, then you will come to the great secret, which is our fire, and these are the material intermediaries purified by the ingenuity of nature, in which there are real, intense, and virtuous beings, tempered through all their accidents, by which you must always temper the stone in its noble mixture and intention through fermentation.

On keeping secret the discovery of the stone.
CAP. XXXIX.
Keep secret that in all things created and formed by nature, in

--- Page 167 (page 75, Theorica Cap. XXXIX cont.) ---

their ultimate purification, there is found the prime matter of all things and of each of them in the form of Mercury. And we see this by noble experience, if you wish to do it. This matter is called the most general genus, not however that you should take it as laymen take it, who take it through a considered term, or through a phantastic being, but as it is a real being, which is the material terminus of nature, from which it receives its more proximate material principles in the action of generation, because it is the first being, which is the material terminus in nature, and our philosophy under the logical shadow calls it the most general genus, because it is the subject of the first nature, which is divided into three, through which it is the genus of the mineral genus; likewise it is the genus of the vegetable genus; and again it is the genus of the animal genus. And therefore we wish our philosophy to be comprehended from three principal genera in the art of experience, namely, from mineral, vegetable, and animal. Know, son, that many will read our books and will not be able to understand them, because in them the principal terms of our true understanding will be unknown and hidden and covered over, and of others likewise, who through their ignorance and

--- Page 168 (Theorica Cap. XXXIX cont. / Cap. XL) ---

deficiency will labor day after day to reprove us, because we speak the truth, and in the end all things will be known along with their reproof. Therefore, if you are able to reach our books hidden from the hands of such people, keep them, and especially from those who practice this art. In which books we openly reveal the entire secret. And above all, guard the present book from the enemies of the world, because great wonders are and will be found therein through certain experiments, as we shall say in the second part of this book.

How the stone after its discovery receives assistance from other things to produce the complete elixir.
CAP. XL.
Since we have further told you that the said genus should be guarded from all black combustion, this is necessary for you, because through that genus you will perfect your entire work with the assistance of the tinctures of the sun and the moon. But it is necessary that you know how to extract it through the art and science of our mastery, as we shall tell you in the second part, and to strip it of all its extrin-

--- Page 169 (page 76, Theorica Cap. XL cont.) ---
THEORICA.
extrinsic figures, which nature placed in the magisterium, such as the figures of diverse plants, and of diverse minerals, and of diverse liquids. And among all of these, nevertheless it has been found more closely in some than in others, and of greater abundance and adherence to the proper substances and natures of metals, according as they contain more of the spirit of the fifth essence. As among vegetable things, the vine — called masculine matter — and great lunaria, which is the vital juice, and the root of wheat or of straw, and purslane and mallow, and Mercurialis and celandine, and other trees and herbs that are of a hot and moist nature, which bear flowers and gums from which oil can be made, whose kind is greatly joined to the matter and nature of metals and minerals, and all other things that are hot and moist by nature. And among beasts there is the species of the basilisk, and of bees that make honey and wax. And among liquids there are human blood and the hair of young men, and urine, the milk of animals, and other viscous moistures. And among minerals there are the sun and moon, because they are the ferments of stones. And from these the said kind came forth most closely, which better adheres

--- Page 170 (Theorica Cap. XL cont.) ---

to the proper substances and natures of metals, namely, gold and silver. From which you must make the fermentation of the stone through natural conjunction, and afterward it will have ingress into every other metal by means of the ferment, which draws it into a nature close to the true medicine, which participates in the essence of perfect bodies and in the essential corruption of perfect things. And through this it has been revealed to you that the more it approaches nature by degrees, the better it participates in nature, and the more it participates, the more quickly by love of nature it joins itself to it and is converted into it. Because wise nature more quickly rejoices and delights with its own nature than with a foreign one. And thus it appears that through the means of nature you must pass gently to the extremities, and draw our stone from potency to act.

On the termination and end of the stone in diverse figures. And how it is among stones, and among salts, and among glasses, all of which came forth from its own nature. And how it must be born.

--- Page 171 (page 77, Theorica Cap. XLI) ---
THEORICA.
And how it must be born.
CAP. XLI.

In this noble nature is terminated and finished the kind (told to you above) which we have drawn from stones, from herbs and animals, in the form of clear water impregnated by the spirit of the fifth essence, which we afterward congeal with the vapor of its sulfur, and then the stone appears which before was hidden from us in its liquorous homogeneity. Son, our sulfur has been created among stones, and it grows among salts and among glasses from the first substance, from the first thing, which is the said substance of quicksilver. And not that we make our entire work from the substance of quicksilver itself, because it is an incorruptible seal, but not from salt, which is not terminated in rock, nor from glass, nor from the substance of any other stone, but among these we create it, because we believe them to be the vessels of nature which the said kind has chosen. And therefore, when our stone shall be created, make it be born from the belly of its mother, and from then on add nothing else, because it carries with it the virtue of the belly of its mother, namely that sulfureous nature which congeals all quicksilver.

--- Page 172 (Theorica Cap. XLI-XLII) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
That is the earth in which we sow our gold, because it has the power to retain it. And you must have such intention in the extraction of the stone from natural principles, both of stones and of herbs: since glorious God placed many virtues in stones and herbs, through which, if they were well known, many marvels would be made from them upon the earth, which simple people would without doubt consider miracles.

On the great conjunction. And on the manner of the male and the female. And on the eclipse of the sun and moon. And on the second birth of our quicksilver, for making the complete elixir.

CAP. XLII.

Now the place has been revealed to you whence our quicksilver can come forth, which makes the conjunction of the female and the male. Do not believe that it is in the place of the female or of the male in the great magisterium; rather it is as it were the mediator of the sun and the moon, since without this quicksilver they would not be well conjoined in the perfection of the man, who requires a receptacle

--- Page 173 (page 78, Theorica Cap. XLII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
of the opportune — that is, the female, who carries him within her belly. And this will be the moon, which will be impregnated by the fire of our sun, until she is black like coal. This our Mercury does, which dissolves the tincture of the sun, which is more red than the blood of the dragon, and it remains entirely tinged. Afterward the female comes, who conceives all of this, and thence it is fermented until delivery. Therefore, son, let it be most clearly revealed to you that the generation of our virtuous stone of great value cannot be made unless the male is joined with a suitable female. For although without the female the said composite of the sun can be dissolved, nevertheless action and passion cannot exist in nature without an agent and a patient in the act of generation and corruption. And through this we make you know that according to the termination of the degrees of nature it composes and decomposes, so that Mercury in the first degree of its conjunction is as much in the act of corruption of that with which it is mixed, until they have been beheaded, namely both Mercury and the sun, and Mercury and the moon. But the moon looks to the act of generation for making the great stone, namely when Mercury is conjoined with her,

--- Page 174 (Theorica Cap. XLII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
then it is nothing but one composite, truly black like coal. Wherefore then it appears that our Mercury is not a suitable female, since the body is of such great purity that through its great clarity it corrupts the composite body, and has the power of separating its own nature from the foreign nature that comes from the elements in their conjunction. On account of this, it needs another noble female, who has the power through proximity of receiving and carrying and nourishing our dear infant without loss of its noble matter — and therefore she turns black, because she wishes to retain it all. Then you will be able to say, when you see this, that the moon suffers an eclipse over the whole earth which she carries in her belly, and the sun likewise, since nothing of it is seen, because both suffer eclipse. And thus we have seen. Whence there are many astrologers who refuse to believe, saying that this is against nature, and in this they speak truly. But they do not have understanding of nature, because it is against nature to corrupt a composite which nature herself created through her great diligence. Therefore it is necessary, son, that the said female be substantial in

--- Page 175 (page 79, Theorica Cap. XLII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
nature, and close to the mineral kind, in respect of all the accidents of the whole universal substance with respect to the male in complexion. For from flesh comes flesh, and from water comes wind, and from wind comes rain. And therefore it is necessary that we have two material and principal substances, which agree to one nature, and are most closely approximating to it, so that the passive nature, of its full will and of its own nature, has the appetite for receiving the form of the masculine virtue. Just as a woman desires a man by instinct of nature more than any other thing. Nevertheless, it cannot be done through a simple female, since she has come and descended from the most general kind, from which all other things have been generated along a line of degrees: but rather with many operations and with long digestion, in which many and almost infinite species would be required, since our work is not yet begun. And when the stone through subtle artifice shall have been created from the nature of metal with the said exuberant quicksilver — for it is necessary that our Mercury be mixed with the nature of two bodies, and afterward be cast out from them — before the complete elixir can be made from it.

--- Page 176 (Theorica Cap. XLII-XLIII) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
And therefore the modern Artists are deluded, who believe they are at the end when they should be beginning, and they make projection with great abuse, because they have not completed the whole course of nature, which lasts for the space of two years complete. Whence the stone is of one year, and the elixir of the other, for every new Artist who has never done it before. But for every good and experienced Artist who is skillful, one year and three months suffice, because according as it is corrupted, so likewise is it generated. Therefore, if he is experienced in its corruption, so much the more will he be experienced in its creation, since that which corrupts is the same thing that creates, and all of this is done through the operation which we call solution and congelation, which we shall give to you through the skilled practice known by us in the second part of this testament.

On the radical natures for achieving and more quickly attaining perfection, and the more special nature of matrimony. And on the virtue that comes forth from this matrimony.

Cap.

--- Page 177 (page 80, Theorica Cap. XLIII) ---

CAP. XLIII.

By this, son, since our masculine sun requires a more appropriately suited female in nature, therefore we choose a more cooked material, through which cooking its nature is turned into the nature of metal, by means of which our magisterium is better completed with great perfection. Therefore, son, you must choose and know the better mineral roots of nature, which are more approximated and to be approximated in natural fusibility with all their ingenuity. With which take care beforehand that you keep our magisterium secret, because the generating agent actively, and the generated thing passively, must be in one species and in a co-similar form, namely, a metallic form, because nature so requires, as we shall speak more fully at the end of our Theorica for better understanding the perfection of our magisterium, which is compared to human generation, by reason of the distinction of its sex or kind. But although the said mineral female is of a hotter nature than the first, which has nothing of complexional heat or cold except in an excellently simple degree, the said

--- Page 178 (Theorica Cap. XLIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

mineral female is cold in nature complexionally with respect to her masculine counterpart, who is hotter in act than she is according to the natural degrees in the complexion of generation. And thus according to diverse actions introduced through nature, diverse forms have been produced in diverse complexions from potency into act. And therefore if the female were joined and added to the first simple female, which descends from the most general kind by intention, so that generation from those two might occur, the mineral female in this case would be the said masculine by reason of its great heat, and would hold the place of the masculine, but is not such as the perfect masculine agent, who is the giver of form through the action of generation, and in whom is the perfect generative form, which carries the matter into complete being. And because although in the moon there is an active and material form, it is nevertheless remote up to a certain degree of gradation, and it is fitting to give to it such an active material thing that has the power to give it a certain spiritual form, because otherwise it would be too slow in the generation of its like. And to avoid such an error, nature shows us a certain experience, by which its virtues must be augmented. Whence we say to you

--- Page 179 (page 81, Theorica Cap. XLIII cont.) ---
THEORICA.

that this is done by the heat of its masculine, both in the first conjunction of their dissolution and in the second reduction of its seed, then copiously and in great abundance our female conceives in her moist womb by the heat of her masculine, which she afterwards willingly returns with great fattening and thick embracing in its reduction, and this through the attraction and appetite which nature took from the heat of her masculine in the first conjunction. Of this we give an example, as you can see from the waters which flow and run down through the conduit of strong mineral virtues, by which they water and imbibe themselves, and through those virtues are converted into stones, sooner or later, according to the mineral virtue, as it has been commingled and strengthened or weakened through its place. And therefore we strengthen the virtue of our female with the heat of her masculine, so that more easily and without force, but with a friendly embrace of nature, she may ferment her first seed and draw it out of her womb, convert it, and congeal it through the heat of her masculine, through which heat the said seed comes into a strong and black exuberance, and then nothing is to be done in the transmutation of nature, because the virtue

--- Page 180 (Theorica Cap. XLIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

of the body of our female, being strengthened, retains and alters the nourishment with material heat, and through the attractive virtue infused into it by the concordance of nature, draws it to itself, and thus it is thickened through the subtle vapor of sulfur, which is in the mineral nature in the likeness of dry and sulfurous Mercurial fume, in whose belly is a fire that neither scorches nor burns, which is a neighbor and friend of nature. And therefore we say that gold is more pleasing to our quicksilver than any other metal, as the attraction of its pure nature makes manifest, by whose virtue our quicksilver is purely congealed into a very pleasing sulfur. Wherefore we say to you that where this virtue is, there is properly the certain place of the generation of our sulfur and of the transmutation of our own seed.

How generation cannot come from qualities
that are too remote. And on the difference
that is between the masculine
and the feminine. And on
the philosophical heat.

CAP. XLIIII.

--- Page 181 (page 82, Theorica Cap. XLIIII) ---
THEORICA.

After these things, son, it can be sufficiently declared to you that from a female that is too cold and a masculine that is also too hot, generation cannot occur, on account of the extremity and intemperance of their qualities. Therefore you must take another female if you wish to perform our magisterium, one that is hotter, and that has a quality of tempered and complexioned coldness, as well as a material moisture, in respect to the proportion of the heat of her masculine, according to what the natural body requires. For from such a proportion the generative virtue is patently brought forth from potency into act. Do you wish therefore, son, that we tell you in how many things you must know the virtue, quality, and quantity of the philosophical heat? In four things therefore you must know it. In the subject, in which it exists according to nature, which is quicksilver. For according to the quality of the material, or its property, it receives its proper determination, as is clear in the elements, in which the secret exists according to nature, and in that which proceeds from the informing virtue, having respect to the celestial virtue, which is the informing virtue of natured nature, and this depends on natural heat. Thirdly, you must consider the virtue of the generative power

--- Page 182 (Theorica Cap. XLIIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

of the simple, and this depends on the heat of simple common fire, well informed. And furthermore you must consider the proportion of this third virtue among the others, namely, that the virtue of the generative power holds captured within itself the formative virtue, that is to say, the artificial heat, directed and governed by the Artist with respect to the celestial virtue, and that the said formative virtue, namely, the exciting artificial heat, holds captured by its great temperance that which is tempered by coldness tempering and moderating the stimulating heat and compelling heat, remaining in the first capture of the composite. Note that the fire of the philosophers consists of four things: namely, the unnatural fire placed in the proper subject of quicksilver, just as the philosopher teaches when he says that fire should properly be placed in bodies, and the simple natural heat of that subject is the informing virtue of heat, and the proportion of these three, because if one exceeds the other, it overpowers it and diminishes it from its action, as appears through digestible heat, namely, that the virtue of the power can operate, and if not, that it operates under the power of the informing virtue and its influence. And that the subject

--- Page 183 (page 83, Theorica Cap. XLIIII cont.) ---
THEORICA.

of the said power is not in excess, and this is achieved through dissolution. Whence note concerning the temperament of the similitudinary waters in the quantity of substance, that is, that it does not exceed the said virtue in power by such an action that the matter is not diminished, and that it is in contrary quality repugnant, and you must know this, that one of the elements must be placed in the other. And as great as the proportion of such power will be in its action, so much the greater will the active product be in operative virtue in its natural operations according to the course of nature. And because, son, if perhaps it will seem impossible to you to know how to perform the magisterium, we shall declare it to you more clearly in the second part, when for doing this we shall have told you that you will add the sun and the moon in equality, and of Mercury more, until all three suffer an eclipse and are in the head and tail of the dragon. Then, son, you will have known unity from plurality, from which nature will make a new generation, when the said unity contains within itself essential plurality, and not accidental, which comes from the part of common Mercury, which is full of accidents. And therefore comes the error of those who deceive men through their

--- Page 184 (Theorica Cap. XLIIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

accidental tinctures, and believe that that is our essential and substantial quicksilver, which indeed has a hundred times greater virtue than the accidental, because essential and substantial operations are nobler than accidental ones, and better in our magisterium. And in that case art deviates from the likeness of nature, because the materials of nature with respect to the materials of art are nothing but accidents, speaking philosophically or moderately. Wherefore it is the work of nature that it must more subtly form all its creatures, and since art cannot operate so subtly, it is necessary that it be aided and assisted according to its power. And this is that matter not so subtle be administered to it, namely through the essential plurality and substantial matter through all its accidents. And the more nature is more essential through the unity of plurality, the more the virtue is of greater majority and magnitude in active and passive essence, nor will the said virtue have in itself any operation or any essential subject in which it could well sustain itself without such action and passion. And therefore such plurality is naturally desirable in our magisterium.

--- Page 185 (page 84, Theorica Cap. XLV) ---
THEORICA.

How from the unity of our quicksilver, which is made from plurality, there proceeds a simple assimilative virtue of the stone.

CAP. XLV.

From this unity, son, made in plurality, there proceeded a certain noble potency and one secret natural principle, which through its simplicity transmits forth from itself out of the first real beings toward the end, so that in them its secrets may be manifested. And thus through secondary simples the first special potencies are revealed, conjoined into one, which we name chaos, nature, origin, green lion, quicksilver, ointment, oil, pasture, and liquor of great value. This is so because many virtues are united into one by the power of nature, governed through the art of the magisterium. And from it proceed secondary operations, which tend toward generation. Because the end of the aforesaid real beings is in the end of the first simples, and thus it is in the whole course of nature, according as one real being of it

--- Page 186 (Theorica Cap. XLV cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
through its noble substance and its dignity has more or better virtue than another. And the greater its virtue, the greater and better is its operation in virtue and potency. And therefore in sophistical operations there can be revealed to every inquirer of this science and searcher of the secrets of nature, and the nobilities of their secrets which are in real beings, as has been said, if he knows how to consider them, through which knowledge he will be able to arrive at our great secret, provided that he first has knowledge of material substances, from which real beings proceed from potency into act. And therefore, son, know which species are of greater virtue, and those under which the individual parts were more virtuous with respect to their own kind. And therefore some individuals are in the nature of minerals that have greater and nobler operation than others, as are first the sun, then the moon, and thus according as the virtue is greater in one kind than in another, so its operation is more virtuous in the one than in the other. And therefore do not work except in the said two luminaries, and in their mediator, who is our Mercury, because their operation is nobler

--- Page 187 (page 85, Theorica Cap. XLV cont.) ---
THEORICA.
and of greater virtue in its species than in any other. And when you will see that the plurality of the two is converted into unity, or the whole into one kind through the power of the active principle and the simplicity of our said matter, then all everywhere participate in one in the ends of the species, and in this principle of the kind and in this unity the ends of the first individuals will begin, which nature has pacified. And therefore it is necessary that the corruption of these be begun and initiated into the dissolution of their new kind, in which their first matter or nature is preserved. Whence it is plainly evident that without simplicity there would be no potency and dominion in the course of nature, and without power there would be no species, and without species there would be no individuals, because it is necessary that they have being under one proper species. And thus it has been revealed to you, son, that with intense simplicity all real beings and their natures move themselves, and also the phantastic ones, and the greater their motion is, the better you can recognize that the natural virtue is of greater power, and consequently its operations, and therefore in the greatness and virtue of motion, the greatness and virtue of its opera-

--- Page 188 (Theorica Cap. XLV cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
tion is multiplied. Moreover, you will have knowledge of the greater virtue if you know how to recognize its greater end, because motion comes from potency, and potency from simplicity — understand this corporeally and naturally. And simplicity comes from subtlety, and subtlety comes from dissolution, which is the end of the corruption of old or ancient individuals. Therefore we say that through dissolution our stone is drawn from potency into act, since from it descend concrete real beings, which are the instruments of all phantastic and real natures through the confusion of noble mixture, and thence come all noble agents. These are the greater motions, and in noble patients, in which their own proper agents are always moved. And when the motions have greater virtues toward agents, whence they receive birth and origin immediately, than they have in patients, by so much is their operation of greater virtue in the active than in the passive. And therefore the sun is of greater virtue, and has a greater instinct of generation, and of more quickly creating something like itself, by reason of its motion, than the moon or any other planet in the whole course of nature, just as a man has greater appetite than a woman, and the soul

--- Page 189 (page 86, Theorica Cap. XLV-XLVI) ---
THEORICA.
better than the body. If therefore, son, you know how to appropriate such a male to such a female, you will have a child who will never die; rather, he will make the dead to live, and after death he will live with them.

How the Artist ought to consider the potencies of the two quicksilvers after their conjunctions, and the differences of their being and of it with respect to their effects.

CAP. XLVI.

Afterwards, son, you must not be ignorant of the potency of these two quicksilvers, namely when they are conjoined together through dissolution, since one is active, the other passive. From these two, through differential concordance, which comes from united potencies and virtues, there proceed four principal natures, which are called by another name the four governing virtues of nature. The first is named the attractive or appetitive. The second nature is the retentive and coagulative. The third nature is the expulsive. The fourth, which is the containing one, is the digestive.

--- Page 190 (Theorica Cap. XLVI cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
The attractive nature is made from the dominion of heat through tempered dryness. The retentive is made through the dominion of dryness. The expulsive is made from the dominion of moisture and constrictive coldness. And the digestive nature is made through putrefaction, which is done through the domination of heat through moderate and tempered moisture. The attractive nature is moved by the hot male, and the retentive by the suffering earth. The expulsive is moved by the humors of the parents, which mixes itself in the said kind that we name quicksilver, which indeed descends more closely from the most general kind. And the digestive is moved by the quality of fire from all things tempered together with their moisture through decoction, mixing all together. And therefore we call the better part of the whole composite, digested and separated, the ointment, the soul, our gold, and oil. The ointment, which colors and tints and melts, and makes all quicksilver flow by reason of its perfection, which it acquired through the said digestion, because in it the cold has been converted into hot, dry into moist, heavy into light, each through its own means, and each through its own mover. Son, if you do not know the differences that are be-

--- Page 191 (page 87, Theorica Cap. XLVI cont.) ---
THEORICA.
tween hot and cold, male and female, you will never know how to perform our work. Know that nothing can be born except from female and male, nor can any sprout be generated except from heat and moisture. The strengths of the appetitive are masculine, hot and dry, and are coagulated fire. The retentive ones are also masculine, cold and dry, and are earth. And the cleansing or digestive strengths, namely the altering ones, are feminine, hot and moist, and are air. The expulsive strengths are likewise feminine, cold and moist, and are water. And the fifth strength is the life of our infant, which is no longer of any quality, namely of coldness, moisture, dryness, and heat, because it is no longer hot, dry, cold, nor moist, neither female nor male. Son, our infant has two fathers and as many mothers; therefore he has been delicately nourished from their whole substance; he reigns and will reign in the world and will not die. Son, the appetitive strengths are hot and dry, and are those which attract, tint, and color, forming colors without separation or alteration, and these proceed from fire, which is their father. The retentive strengths, cold and dry, are those which adjoin colors

--- Page 192 (Theorica Cap. XLVI cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
and heats to bodies, and soften them, binding the said tinctures with a strong bond without any separation. The digestive and cleansing strengths are those which clean and wash the impurities of bodies, and from them extract the nature and constitute and arrange it burning, kindled, and illuminated, because they are hot and moist. The expulsive strengths, cold and moist, are those which expel the vices of the world, and when necessary, they transmute bodies among themselves together until they have passed into their crude nature, and afterwards when each of the elements has been turned into one, they will never be separated nor divided. And just as each of them is extracted from a body, so also it is turned into a body, and thus through art they compose themselves and are added into one, and their natures are conjoined. But the fifth virtue is that which does the whole, and leads and conducts all things to the end of its composition, and it is the spirit of the fifth essence infused by nature into the substances of composites, to the end that its virtue through art may be reflected and fixed in those bodies.

--- Page 193 (page 88, Theorica Cap. XLVII) ---
THEORICA.

How the stone receives diverse names through its diverse effects.

CAP. XLVII.

Son, when you wish to begin our magisterium, do not work except with the natures that shine most brightly. From which most brightly shining natures the lesser world was created, which we shall name for you by their properties. Know therefore, son, that sulfur is fire, and magnesia is our most precious earth, and secondarily it is our air, and quicksilver is our living water, which flows and runs through the entire body. But the secret spirit is the second water, from which every thing must receive nourishment, and every seed its vegetation, and every light its illumination. If, however, you desire to perform the operation masterfully and through art without error, it is necessary that in natural coequality you bring together all these natures, namely the hot and the cold, the moist and the dry, the male and the female, masterfully. From this conjunction is made clear the begin-

--- Page 194 (Theorica Cap. XLVII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
ning of the generation of the stone, and afterwards you shall nourish it through water, which carries within itself light and life and spirit, cooking your work equally with steady perseverance, constancy, and stability, and compose it according to the composition of those things from which it wishes to be composed, namely, the sun and the moon, and our Mercury. Son, our composite is founded from diverse natures, for the body and its quantity arise from melancholy. Then fire, which is red choler, is composed and added to the heart or to the liver, to properly direct that which comes to the heart. After this digestion, the matter digested by fire is expelled and thrust to the liver, to which air is added, which is the blood flowing and running through all the veins of the body, and then the color appears, when it has been entirely turned to red. And the water that we call phlegm is placed in the lung and ascends to the tongue and to the other instruments of the mouth. Thus, son, it is necessary for you, through the science and measure of the art, to add natures until the bodies have been made into spirits through the accidental strengths introduced in the course of our philosophy through the art of the magisterium. Add therefore two natures until you have drawn forth the entire nature pure and shining and clear, for from

--- Page 195 (page 89, Theorica Cap. XLVII-XLVIII) ---
THEORICA.
those two one clear thing must be born, which must illuminate its entire kind. Guard the bodies, however, in their liquefactions; when you are at work, do not exceed the measure that the signs of nature will show you in each decoction, because nature is changed into nature until the bodies remain in it, permanent in liquefaction and in fusion, on account of the permanence of the fixed materials, which are our secrets, well tempered and moist, into which the bodies have been converted through the art of the magisterium, and they are unguents, oils, ferments, and souls, and suns that do not cease to shine upon our earth and to radiate. Therefore it is necessary for you to fix in the attenuation of nature, to form the body of philosophy, until it becomes a clear water, which water is called the water of wisdom, for it is entirely gold and silver, carried into the action of the spiritual elements, in which the spirit of the fifth essence rests and rejoices, which makes everything.

How the Artist and operator must guard the nature of the spirit of the fifth essence as the form of forms. And how he must understand it. And on the measure of fire.

--- Page 196 (Theorica Cap. XLVIII) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

And how he must understand it. And on the measure of fire.

CAP. XLVIII.

Therefore, son, it is necessary for you to guard the nature of the spirit of the fifth essence, if you wish to accomplish anything, which indeed depends upon the first cause, as the form of forms, which has placed itself in the said four more honorable natures, and is named the soul of the elements, the clear light, and the spirit of the fifth essence. And because every substantial formed thing descends immediately from this proximate fifth cause, we call it the mother of mothers of all other forms. Son, you must not understand that this form and spirit is hot, cold, dry, or moist, nor male or female, but understand that on account of the greatness of its perfection it reaches to each of those four natures, for without this spirit there would be no complexion in any complexioned thing, as we manifestly know and see with certain experience in the binding and complexion of the four elements, which indeed with contrary quali-

--- Page 197 (page 90, Theorica Cap. XLVIII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
ty oppose one another, by which opposition the said contrary elements could not agree so as to return into one, unless there were that most noble medium, which goes confusedly through all the elements and participates in each of their natures. Therefore, son, if you wish to know this, it is that which puts peace between enemies, and to it one must attend. And by way of example, air and water are between two enemies: air between the hot and the cold, and water likewise since it is between the moist and the dry, that is, between air and earth. And therefore when we wish that the dry be converted into the moist, we take the instrument that is in water, which indeed participates through its moisture with the radical moisture, that is, the hereditary and aerial vapor, carried in its aquatic phlegm, in which the fifth spirit has been borne. Without which vapor, which is the matter of our seed carried in its phlegm that descends from the said most general genus, it could not be congealed. Therefore it has been said to you that you must know well how to dispose the aerial substance of our stone, and the terrestrial substance of the same stone, which is its mother, until they are well conjoined without separation. Son, the aerial substance is the matter of our seed,

--- Page 198 (Theorica Cap. XLVIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
which is otherwise called permanent water, whose moisture is very hot, because it is clothed with the property of the fire of nature. And therefore it is necessary that you know how to dispose it and to know upon what earth you must gather it. For therein lies the entire force, and all the virtues of our stone lie there. For if the substance of the seed were congealed and converted in the belly of its mother, which is the vessel of nature, except through the spirit having exhaled its aeriality, which participates in its essence by the virtue of stimulating heat, the seed would remain without a soul, nor would it any longer have a pulse or motion of any breath. And thus it would be mortified in the manner of earth in a white color, which would need a vivifying spirit, namely air, in which every thing is vivified and inspired. Therefore our earth without this spirit would bear no fruit, for this spirit perfects it. Be willing, then, son, to congeal the volatile aeriality in its earth, with the loving nourishment of an appropriate fire, just as an infant in the arms of its mother, namely, that the said fire be proportioned in such a degree that it does not overcome the force of the hot and moist spirit. For the hot nature of this spirit would quickly be dissolved by the flame of an improper fire,

--- Page 199 (page 91, Theorica Cap. XLVIII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
just as the power of a great flame annihilates the power of a small one. And you must in this case imitate nature, namely, that you take from elemental fire in the generation of things of nature only insofar as you will need it for the proportion of the excitation of the fire of nature, which moves through the entire matter. And from such a likeness to nature, in which is the generative virtue, she is your directress and teacher. Therefore know how to understand her. For she herself, provided with great wisdom and great diligence in her natural work, takes from elemental fire only as much as she needs for the information of her matter. Thus, son, you must fix and implant the said spirit in the earth, drying it carefully through the heat that we call escatesis, for it endures the sense of nature, because it suffices that the dryness of the earth be sustained by the dryness of a very feeble and thin fire. And with all this, through the subtle heat of the said fire, its own nature may be excited, without exceeding its virtue, because through that virtue the hot spirit penetrates and enters to the most profound depths of the honorable matter, just as the soul enters the body of a man to give life. Son, this is the truest aerial substance, which you must through natural skill

--- Page 200 (Theorica Cap. XLVIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
compel and fix in the body, lest it remain mortified. And after this, cast out its phlegm with all superfluities, and then you will have the terrestrial substance well disposed through moisture and heat, without separation of one from the other, and entirely converted to the nature of air, which binding was made through the said water, which is the medium of the dry and the moist, and is called the inceration of nature, or natural inceration, because nature makes it through the generation and vivification of mortified things, when through sublimation it joins the dry with the moist, and conversely. For this mixture is of nature, because it is made with fire, and without fire it is not. Therefore it is necessary that the sulfur be composed of very subtle terrestrial matter, strongly digested, and of subtle aerial heat, as in the vessel of nature, in which the said moist and the said terrestrial matter are mixed together subtly, and through the smallest and imperceptible particles, so that the minimum of the terrestrial dry and the minimum of the moist be gathered together in the quantity of one grain of one and in a similar quantity of the other, in such a way that neither one nor the other is separated from the parts of its kind, as manifestly appears through their sublimation.

--- Page 201 (page 92, Theorica Cap. XLVIII cont.) ---
THEORICA.
manifestly. In which certain and proven experience shows that this whole either flies and ascends, or remains entirely below. And thus through art it is found that no part is separated from another, after nature has bound them together with the greater and the lesser, and with great and wondrous diligence. Son, all this takes place in the first creation of our stone, and it is the mixing of other things — that is, the union of other living silvers — which can be bound together in the essential unity of the spirit of the fifth essence through alteration, so much so that they will never afterward be separated from their homogeneities, as will later be seen through the experience of sublimation, which can gather together that which is of one kind. After this you must not be ignorant that it is necessary for our medicine to be composed and to have been composed from one nature and one kind.

How sublimation separates the pure and clean from the impure and unclean, through the subtlety of the ingenuity of art and nature. And how you should understand

--- Page 202 (Theorica Cap. XLIX) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI
the soul. And what the soul is.

CAP. XLIX.

Afterward, son, our sublimation separates all that is pure and clean, which has been collected by Mercurial unity, from the gross, earthy, and unclean dregs.
