CAPVT XXXVI.
Qualiter debes intelligere sublimationem Mercurij, & quanta & qualis est differentia inter sublimationem vulgi primo, & de sublimatione philosophorum. Et de falsis reprobatis ludeis mercedis & surrectorum.

The sublimation of Mercury, son, what manner and what kind of difference we understand, and all the philosophers [understand], is its decoction: namely that one part of it is turned into sulfur, and another part into quicksilver, which gives omnipotent life through the property by which [it is] to be transmuted into our quicksilver, on account of the depravation toward it of the binding [power]. Son, if you wish to understand our sublimation, you must consider through four [things], namely the nature of simple fire and to digest and to sublimate, because sublimation and decoction are one and the same, inasmuch as sublimation [involves] separation from the property. And to simplify and digest the material

--- Page M78 (printed page 80, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVI cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

matters through diverse decoctions, which it receives from fire excited outside of nature through common fire, in such a manner that the heat of nature which makes all things fruitful, not [that] it may sufficiently be separated from the said matter, but rather it is necessary, and it is needful that you cause [it] to be sublimated, to ascend and to be exalted, and to vivify the quicksilver by sublimating and digesting beyond the natural decoctions, which the matter and its nature may receive, and as we have said concerning the fire against nature. And therefore you must liken yourself to every wise physician, who must strengthen nature. And hiding nature under a cloak, to see clearly, and in pressed [matters], and in other hardnesses it appears, it is necessary that he use many digestive [agents], and divisible [ones], and [that] the hard matter of the medicine be subtiliated [and] offered to nature. Secondly, it is necessary that he consume it and use a well-laxa-

--- Page M79 (printed page 81, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVI cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

laxative [medicine], so that the digested matter may be expelled outward and conveniently evacuated. Thirdly, it is said to be necessary [to use] certain strengthening [agents] to strengthen nature, which has been made weak and debilitated by the violence of the medicine in [its] restoration. And fourthly, that the work be given with certain restorations and strengthening [agents] and restorations, and that which nature has lost through deficiency in its authority may be restored. Similarly, the operator in our regimen must do [the same] in his operations. Because through the first operation he must understand L and K, and through the second L M N O P Q R, and through the third he must understand the reduction of M and L, and in O through the fourth the reduction of N, so that what went out from L M and O. When you wish to have N to P through M from L. And you must use these evacuations, and through a great length with patience, since E of the philosophizing [ones] is of the number of the mixed [substances]. And that the natural properties of vegetability may not recede from L except by strong evacuation

--- Page M80 (printed page 82, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVI cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

Therefore, because evacuation [that is] too sudden is abrupt, and also too often repeated [is] lofty in effect before the refrigeration of the matter, [it is] impossible, utterly harmful, deceptive, and inimical to nature. Son, L is not evacuated from N except because it brings, desires, and seeks to draw M. M, having effected the digestion of L, would have converted [it] into its own nature. But L seizes [it] implicated if through the derivation of N from M, [it is] denuded from N [and] L, which impeded the love of L for M. And therefore you can understand in a threefold [manner] that three things are principally necessary for our sufficient sublimation. First, dissolution. Second, consumption. Third, restoration. Dissolution is for emptying things that are full: constriction is for diminishing things that are dissolved: and restoration is for replenishing things and [for] restoration. Restoration, however, is said to be done gradually without precipitation. And that convalescence and temperament [should follow], because nature in its empty and evacuated vessels is accustomed to have a multitude of lesser [things] and appetite. And

--- Page M81 (printed page 83, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVI cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

and therefore it would receive more than it could exchange and digest, unless through a discrete operation it held to the regimen of diet. And therefore we say in our Theory, if you wished to understand us, that [the process] naturally converts a small amount, sufficiently small, gradually dissolved into parts, rather than a gross [amount] dissolved all at once. And if our Mercury [is] sublimated and purged through digestions and the communication which the fire of nature makes with the remaining parts divided through the four elements. When therefore you wish our Mercury to be habituated, inasmuch as the four elements [are] configured, whether the property be solid or not solid, in which it can sense nothing of its own [nature] that is not extrinsic, except only that which is contrary to it for corrupting its elements, it could approach its aforesaid elements. And from these elements it may refound the composed matters, which it may lead forth and be able to fly. And in its fire it may concoct in the space of time

--- Page M82 (printed page 84, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVI cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

of time, which is required in nourishing with its milk, just as a child [is nourished] safely at the breasts of its nature, it could thus go to its head, that which they could [draw] from the air, of which it would have great need, and thus it would recover the power of penetrating every body and [fixing] it notably in H or E or F. Sublimation, son, by one voice prevails over all operations: particularly and operatively. Therefore, because according to the work, sometimes sublimation stands for all the operations required and performed in the great matter of perfection. And sometimes sublimation is taken for the declaration of each individual operation, whether for drawing out by dissolution alone, or through the dry [way] or as induration. And if the said operation has been performed with the intention of great perfection, insofar [as it] would pertain to the regimen of the said operation. The useful sublimable [substance] thence, and of the resurrected [ones] toward it [there] was not [such deception], [but] thus the deceived [ones], as appears in their damnable notes and letters, namely in the epistle

which indeed baptism is one of the principal means of salvation, just as is found in the writings through the Apostle, through the most blessed Marchaliet and from the books of Crestia to be brought forth. Where it is said in their Hebrew language Ramicha ya his haeresi serem maym haanae unta thateton colombam ya dehamischa, that is to say, grace, and through truth, you [are] well ordered, that these things are, and the secrets of the sacraments shall pass as a declaration over you, and shall place [you] outside the abysses, and you shall raise the right hand over all of you, and you shall make a sign in justification. And elsewhere [there are] many [things] to be proven through their scriptures where it is thus written, and it is said that Elijah the prophet [spoke]:
[HEBREW TEXT]
[HEBREW TEXT]
You [shall go] to the bathing in the confections of waters, in great gladness, the fountains of salvation, rightly. And the sacred [things] for the Lord's prayer, according to what their scriptures demonstrate, and teach, without [need for] lofty gloss they would be saved. But

--- Page M89 (printed page 93, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVI cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

and what that thing is which they make concerning the quality of prayer against all Christians, which they cry out (de ros adonai) — let it be against them, because it is nothing but their own iniquity; nor what it was that was condemned in their Mosaic tongue against all of us Christians. And because we have spoken at length about all these things, and about their many deceptions, combined defections, and about all their most abominable errors in the book of the Reformation of the Hebrews, there, my son, you will find things through which all of them are rightly condemned in secret and it is revealed concerning them, that it manifestly appears that by the very clearest right of profession it is shown to them that devout but foolish Jews and pagans cannot fully enter into the work, and their affairs, unless they are in the scriptures. And because there is no end for them in this, nor concerning the thing which we have said above [?], one can supply from that which is done only through the light and clarity of the divine spirit.

--- Page M90 (printed page 94, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVI cont. / Caput XXXVII) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

Therefore it is not said nor clarified in quantity, nor to the Jew, since all things fail us from the light of given clarity to the people of Christianity before the desolation and the elucidated truth to the people of Christianity. And woe indeed to the waters, which they give you without the judgment of darkness, while you wish to seize them, just as one must proceed step by step, for the Lord would give them here to you of his own, nothing but the luminous and clear property which is present. And wherever you shall have been far from your enemies, it shall be directed for them.

CAPVT XXXVII.
On the diversity of fire against nature according to the property of the nature of each of them in formation. And how they share one consolation.

We say to you, son, that you can work with body alone, or with quicksilver alone, or with both together, more certainly and with diverse intentions, which if you must have as practices according to how you shall

--- Page M91 (printed page 95, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVII cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

work either with body or with spirit or with both together. Because if you have the intention of working our stone from body alone, you can do it, and this by the intention of retrograde decoction, which the nature of the metal has received, by leading back and reducing it into the crude menstrual nature. And if you have the true intention concerning Mercury or quicksilver alone, from that you can likewise make the stone with such intention, that what is exceedingly crude may be turned by nature into the cooked menstrual state. This was the composition worked by fire into the diversity of heaven, afterwards because each of these must pass through four operative means, of which four there are, the end of each one, which resides in the philosophy of stones through operation. And if thus there are seven operative means, namely B C D E E G H: by B is signified the operation of liquefaction, by

--- Page M92 (printed page 96, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

by C solution and elementation, by D elementation and congelation, by E our sublimation, by F elementation and congelation, by G fusion and elementation, by H liquefaction. By B C D E they are made through the intellectual element of Mercury. By E F G H the operations of the body are understood, and it is done through retrogradations, and by the joined letters to be named slightly through retrogradations, thus H G F E. And if the position is to be supposed in E. But when the body is turned from E into O, for so much Mercury does not have the power of the true degree, being already touched from B into C or from C into D. Because the body will more quickly turn back to what it was, because just as the heat of Mercury most naturally burns, yet settling into the beginning or state of its nature, and more quickly the destructive or more immutable thing destroys clean things, than lightly or it moves unclean things, and therefore every diverse thing is more quickly torn apart

--- Page M93 (printed page 97, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVII cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

by the separation of impure parts, which tend toward corruption, and of pure parts which seek reformation. And therefore the fire of Mercury, of which there will be both, was of a nature sympathetic with the other nature which through its decoctions has beginnings and principles, and by reason of its heaviness and ponderousness and crudity and grossness. And because through the nature of the elements it cannot be turned by its own resolutions into true resolutions, being desiccated it needs decoctions for attenuating, for lightening its ponderous weight, for overcoming its crudity, and thus the most finite of its grossness, so that it may arrive at perfection from itself through the succession of the reversion of re-incrudation. Then understand, son, that after passing through D into E, then it returns from E, and from B into C, and from C into H, and from there to K, and from K into L. These letters, son, are of great understanding, and with them one must understand

--- Page M94 (printed page 98, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

that when E wishes to be graduated into F, and from F into G, etc., then F G H take on other significations, for by F one understands the liquefactions of ferments, and by G the sublimations of ferments, and by H the medicinal elementation and congelation, by I the multiplication and quantity, and by L the meditations of philosophers on the transmutation of the virtue of the medicine. And all of that you must understand concerning the operation made and perfected from Mercury or from quicksilver. But although, son, we said that Mercury needs perfection, nevertheless you should not — concerning decoctions we say this again for the sake of the thing commensurate and measured by another nature which has greater regard for the more perfect quality and virtue of sulphur, in which every body is produced through the productions made by nature. But if the measure of the thing is thus commensurate or [?] not thus commensurate, one cannot simply abbreviate the work and trans-

--- Page M95 (printed page 99, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVII cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

passing successively from B into C, and from C into D, and from D into E, just as we have said. But the effect is not so great as when commensurate with the other nature by which its decoctions are made whole, which is more efficacious by the ingenuity of nature. Inasmuch as the fire of the material is made according to the demand beyond what is due to nature, so much virtue and strength is subtracted from it. For virtue and strength do not come into E, if it has had only surface material without substance. And the sublimation of materials goes out and is born from sublimation and rarefaction, and all of that cannot come except through palpable material, and palpability has no other likeness except to that which is of such an element, which air is of the whole thing from P into the rest [?], and all things brought to simplicity. Therefore your Mercury, having been brought to simplicity, must be worked from on high, practiced from on high as material, with great deposition, from which is hoped the pure moisture of clarified humidity. Wherefore let it be declared to you that from Mercury alone or from M. alone or quicksilver you can make the work of your

--- Page M96 (printed page 100, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVII cont. / Caput XXXVIII) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

perfection and imperfection. If it be perfection, it will have been made by cause and reason of decoctions and digestion of good and hard things. But imperfection is made through deviating acts of decoctions and their brevity and not fully whole nor well-made things, because which is the more powerful by reason of its propinquity, weakness and faculty, which was first made, so that such things may have its propinquity and lightness, but are not perfected except through the aid of their complete decoctions, or through a material more mixed with theirs in a perfect body, as we shall afterwards tell you, with the proper places of things concerning the body of incorporation.

CAPVT XXXVIII.
On the retardation of the perfect body. And on its hardness and accidents and weight.

In the above-said, son, you can well and naturally understand how cooked things are made crude

--- Page M97 (printed page 97, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVIII cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

re-incrudation, meets disorderly with the good decoction of the crude according to the course of nature, until the separation of both is turned into the virtue of one, which we call the philosophical stone and medicine. Let this reading be given to fill the interpretation [?], and just as it is to exist together: so nature simultaneously directs those two into one virtuous body, and it looks much upon the fact that one desires a body, and another increases toward mixture and the artifice of effects and operations through the habitual art, for the master seeks by the will of A when nature attains and achieves its end. And therefore if we tell you that just as one domination over these decoctions is given in the passions: so another in its re-incrudations shows how to build diverse powers, such as Mercury, which is gross and terrestrial through its youthful temperament, the earth dominating through the vinegar power, son, in the decoctions of the first. Simply through the contrary

--- Page M98 (printed page 98, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

the perfect body shows its actions and contrary accidents through the noble and great decoction, just as the material demonstrates in its retrograde re-incrudation. And just as this is the cause of its motion in the likeness of the first, so it shows through the contrary sense that it ministers scarcity. And therefore through its good elementation, digestion, and sublimation, the elements revolve and the fixed ones liquefy and consent and endure, which is inclined through volatile properties, if it has not had a restraint described by nature, that is, from the temperament of the proper menstruum with the informative property of Mercury, which nature elevates and puts to flight when they are assailed by malignity, which it could not overcome. And thus just as the spirit is resplendent and full of clarity, so the body is moist, empty, quiet, fused, and dark. And through this body, if the nourishing presence is sustained in obedience and weight, it is

--- Page M99 (printed page 99, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVIII cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

ours. Therefore when you wish to dissolve the best body, congeal the spirit with it if the Mercury is most ponderous, which carries the journey begun. But if so, hear: first let it be reverted into H, and from H into G through re-incrudation, afterwards from G into F through retrogradation, and after F it turns into E, and from E if you wish to roll the stone, you will roll it also by hand. But if it is a body of proper cognition and extracted from the virtue of its own proper substance and from other extraneous greater things. Therefore you should understand that the work of Mercury P is ponderous, subtle, and of nature. And that the body is to be exalted and dissolved and to ascend on high. Therefore let it be shown to you that if both are well prepared together, the works are marvelous without the weight of great exactness, as if from one single leaf much is to be said, since the force

--- Page M100 (printed page 100, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXVIII cont. / Caput XXXIX) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

possesses the other, and one amends and improves the other. And one from the other amends and improves the other. Therefore, son, work the mass of operations from both together, just as we have told you concerning the greater stone, and with the understanding which we shall tell you after these branches of this state, after we have discoursed to you concerning the double co[?]junction, which is called one, although they are two things conjoined and in conjunction, of which the crests are together.

CAPVT XXXIX.
On the double conjunction of body and Mercury, and on their operations in contrary fashion.

When the work of the body and Mercury together is undertaken, then in the mastery many contrary things are apt to occur, through which the work of nature is both amended and strengthened and thickened to such a degree, with a double helm, the bald one variegated. Therefore when it

--- Page M101 (printed page 101, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXIX cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

on account of their qualities they are thus set in opposition to one another in their actions, passions, and operations. Because the spirit naturally seeks fixation on high in the true mastery, and the body seeks the contrary, and desires to fly in the form of clear water, and the congealed spirit remains in the form of white earth, brown and quiet like salt, or crystal, or thick calite. Insofar as it is necessary to aid the spirit which hitherto has not been able, and is naturally separated from the body, and this is done through the water of the body. Therefore understand what the spirit of the virtue of the body does, whose end is corporeal, to coagulate the spirit, as we say one reason out of many, concerning this compendium of the art. Nor indeed is the spirit congealed without the dissolution of the body. Then understand likewise concerning how dissolution is made, and concerning the volatile body falling for some time through some process before the work of constriction, and not of the corpo-

--- Page M102 (printed page 102, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXIX cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

real fixed thing clothed in a black garment, which is the dissolution through constriction and coagulation of its own quicksilver, which is its natural principle, and is silver against nature. Here lie, son, the contrary operations: because just as fire against nature dissolves the body of the fixed spirit into red water, and congeals the body of the volatile spirit into corporeal earth: similarly in the contrary manner, the fire of nature congeals the spirit dissolved from the fixed body into earth. And the body of the volatile spirit fixed by fire against nature dissolves into water, not into red water, but into the water of the philosophers. Therefore because the fixed, by the virtue of the fire of nature, is volatile, and the body is turned into spirit, and the moist into dry, and the heavy into light. Just as by the contrary, by the virtue of fire against nature, the volatile becomes fixed by virtue, and the fixed volatile, and the body into spiri-

--- Page M103 (printed page 103, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXIX cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

tual, and the spirit into body, and the dry moist in the form of ruby water, and the heavy into constriction. Therefore understand thus, that the fire against nature is so called because the property of nature is contrary to all the contractions of nature, the key wishing thus that the other, which the natural composed fire cannot always do, the fire against nature always decomposes and brings to corruption, although it confounds all the complements of the first nature so that they may be dried and whitened. Therefore we tell you that such fire or its operation is not of the virtue and operation of our mastery. Rather it is the corruption of fire which is of natural origin. Therefore let it be done by us. The exaltation of Solomon is of the art of Aries, when you see that the whole material is confused, and therefore it signifies to you that our mastery must begin when the sun is in Aries, that is to say in our work: for then you must

--- Page M104 (printed page 104, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXIX cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

say, know, and discern what is the true understanding, which the philosopher gives you, when he says that first by sublimations and the tartar of the root one must dissolve and conjoin and fortify upon the stone, until you know that it is well exalted and sublimated. And we tell you that it is nothing but fire led from potency into action, by which, lest you be deceived through understanding and the terms of the art, since they are not the works which we have told you. Whence there are many accidents which are caused by contraries through such operations, just as in the properties it has been lightly revealed. Wherefore be very attentive. And for you it does not serve the multitude of masteries when I wish the dissolution to be relinquished without the congelation of the other; you should not doubt whence you may have earth, nor similarly water, in order to be able to make the proper earth which is dry, which true substance confers, congealed and impregnated by the virtue of the body.

--- Page M105 (printed page 109, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXIX cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

of the body and the fire of the sun, through which quicksilver remains philosophically sublimated, as was said above: and the water is of the proper metallic substance, in which the moist substance of quicksilver is made ready, which perfection with respect to or impregnated by the property of the earth with natural fire, in which is the celestial virtue that vivifies and illuminates the earth of Mercury, which is mildly dead. And therefore the reception of that water opens it, receiving the lost thing. But since the thing itself, which is the vivifying agent of the moisture, has been fermented and made abundant through the body, as we have said, therefore it merits transformation and is accounted a work, which by forces or among the multiplication of the moisture's collection: and thus the elements have been brought together by art, and so that it may be brought together through abundance, so that they may be efficaciously mixed with greater things, because if M and P are united with Q, R, and N against L and O, on account of the corruption of Z, which is the cau-

--- Page M106 (printed page 110, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XXXIX cont. / Caput XL) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

se of the dissolution of L and O. And afterwards M in P unite L and O to receive Q, R, and N after the adulterated ones have been made abundant, on account of the admixture of I, which is against Z, just as we have declared in the compendious treatise, both particular and general, of this entire art through abbreviated doctrine, in which we have shown how and in what manner the combined elements are uniting for composing and corrupting every body, with the assistance of I and Z, which are the instruments of corruption and generation of all bodies.

CAPVT XL.
On certain branches or tracts of nature by way of practice, in which, my son, the method of the art may be wrested forth or made solar, and first concerning the lunar body or silver.

--- Page M107 (printed page 111, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XL) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

If you wish to know the transmutation of the proper lunar body in clear practice, it is necessary for you to separate from that quicksilver what is terminated by its earthiness, for that is what gives silver its whiteness, and it is nothing but a terminated moisture in the species of silver, in the virtue of sulfur. Therefore it is necessary that you attend to dissolving the immutable through experiments, which is the contrary of condensation, and that you have such moistures in your reduction as nature has in its condensation, which are now extracted. Take living bodies beginning in the living trunk, and as we have told you, liquefy them in six parts of rectified E, yet without reiteration of calcination. Then place them to ferment in a gentle, temperate heat for eight days, and then removing at the bottom of the vessel the substance of the body, black like charcoal. In this place it is to be considered that it must

--- Page M108 (printed page 112, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XL cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

be whitened through condensation or the humoral with respect to nature, as we have said above, which moisture is first condensing before it had been liquefied in the species and metallic color through the continuation of the parts of the said black sulfur: But after it has been separated from that through liquefaction, place the material again in its bath, and let it be with that liquefied moisture and your elemented elements through the mixture of the instrument of elementation. But first you shall element the water, and afterwards in the elementary water you shall element the parts in the furnace, and you shall congeal them well, as the instrument of vegetation appears to you, and then immediately it shall return its elemented state to its earthy condition, just as above it had been exalted to the igneous state, and through the body by igneous dissolution you shall restore it in the metal: but in white sulfur. Because there is no

--- Page M109 (printed page 113, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XL cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

passage from one extremity to another except through its medium. Son, the converted moisture of fire is earth, but the medium converting quicksilver into metallic form is sulfur in the form of subtle parts created, just as we have told you in the multiplication itself. And thus it is manifest to you that the corruption of quicksilver terminates in the metallic species, since nature through mutual artifice makes for itself that form with that natural sulfur, and the generation of sulfur, and without it nothing will ever be perfected. If therefore quicksilver has not been corrupted or dissolved into the four elements, and your own well subtilized, so that it may be of the most subtle and purest nature, and the quicksilver — two sulfurs are created from the most subtle substance of the living in water, from the clear living body, and from it the possibilities of the mutations of nature, not without the favor of the division of parts. Corrupt the quicksilver of

--- Page M110 (printed page 114, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XL cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

the living, and from its subtle divided parts and the form of subtlety, seek the stone that has the proceeding mastery, and make the living thing by corrupting, and removing it from its own species, and afterwards to the virtue of its material in power and corporating it into another species, namely of sulfur: and thus let it be assimilated to nature, and let nature be formed into that which through operations of certain successive degrees — so long as your works are similar to those by which nature proceeds, and resist proceeding — just as through the said purgations of sulfur and other operations which have been made in the art by decoction and sublimation, as we have told you, if you understand us philosophically, as nature requires, and through such preparation the proper elements of quicksilver, and through the office of elemented fire, and through the purgation of its whole substance divided through the four elements,

--- Page M111 (printed page 115, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XL cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

and through such good permutation, as we have told you, of those two, and with the fermentable matter of the metal. Then, son, truly in this matter through the virtues of sulfur and quicksilver every metallic species shall be introduced, and the living matter of nature — whether it shall make silver — only by this method does the way proceed, since there is but one single operation of nature, and limited by nature: and thus let it make all truths flow, because if you do what is recalled to have been done in matter similar to metal, just as before, since the work has been manifestly shown above, that the quicksilver may be turned into truth or may have been, and afterwards let it be reduced and returned to its first species of aggregation, which we call that from which a new species is formed through generation, which leads them from potency into act. Wherefore let it be fully revealed to you, that concerning those things which through lofty [?] doubt by proceeding through similar circuits

--- Page M112 (printed page 116, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XL cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

through individual corruption, truly such ones are deceivers, nor for all this can they make true gold, nor true silver, except through contrary operations of drawing from silver bodies with the assistance of liquefaction through sublimation, and that through possible admixture it may be done in properties, for on account of such corruptions they cannot make a true tincture to white or to red, until that which has been corrupted is renewed through a new generation. And when the whitened and resplendent body is recalled: then through such a renewed white thing a tincture to white has been made, and through the fire of the stone to red. Therefore, because when the corrupted metal in the said renewals is complete, at no time hereafter can a tincture from that easily come to corruption, since

--- Page M113 (printed page 119, Liber Mercuriorum, Cap. XL cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

because inwardly and outwardly they are purified, and terminated in great lucidity: but there are few who know how to proceed by this way, because they are ignorant of the ways of nature, and therefore the gold and silver of those ones cannot sustain fire on account of the nature of its parts, but rather they are consumed and return to corrupted earth. And this is because when such ones have placed the metal in the course of corruption, it is necessary that it be entirely corrupted, and that it return to dregs under the total loss of its species, because they did not restore the whole body from its corruption, or if they did have it, they did not purge it in its purifications of the corrupted parts: and because those parts remain mixed in their impurity, they are burned and catch fire, and recede throughout their whole species: and this is the cause and reason of combustible and burning sulfurs, because they feel the heat of the fire, insofar as the parts are not conjoined to the individual-

--- Page M114 (printed page 120, Liber Mercuriorum, Cap. XL cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

individuals of the noble species. Therefore, son, let it be recollected to you in summary, that you should make a white or red tincture from no other thing than that which nature has formed, until their corrupted parts have been [removed], and renewed through the art of the mastery. And concerning this we give you a motive for having a brief work, if you know the light with the ferment of the body, because from oil and sulfur a composite is made, which tints all metals into gold or silver, according to whether it is white or red, just as it is written to us, who have seen it, recounted through the work of a certain associate, whom we found going through the world: which associate indeed made known to us many great marvels, but afterwards elaborated in his art, so that we might know more, and by the perception by which he recognized for us, or understood through art: whence afterwards we reformed it in many other things through the reinvention of the senses, and great vir-

--- Page M115 (printed page 121, Liber Mercuriorum, Cap. XL cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

tues up to this point, that we were satiated with experiments, and that we refined the virtuous stone, and for three years we were together in great fidelity, whence at Milan in the third year completed, namely in the year one thousand three hundred and thirty-three, the work was accomplished, of which each of the three of us who knew the art took his part, and the vessels remained a fourth, which remained in its fire, as before regenerating: wherefore many go through the world, knowing many good and pleasing things, and men among them, so that from them you may be able to perceive something of utility, and show them a good countenance, better than to a king, giving them of yours according to good reason, and have over him a certain solicitation, through which you may be able to draw out some thing with a little practice. And let this suffice for you as our testament: to everyone nevertheless we say, that he should be careful of the practice

--- Page M116 (printed page 122, Liber Mercuriorum, Cap. XL cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

to show it ethically: for if he fails in his showing of it, he shall receive himself in his offense against nature. But you, who do not have it, are bound to ask by word whether he is willing to give it to you, and therefore let him beware what he does, because if he makes it too public, he shall die a bad death, because it is to be given by God, who created nature, and not by another. Therefore nature willingly accepts everything that pleases God: and therefore if God should in some manner confer it upon you, love it, and keep it secret, so that the world may know nothing of it, just as the scripture vividly admonishes you, that the work which can be done by the course of nature ought not to be shown to another. It wills only to be secret, so that unless you keep it secret, it shall confound you: And if it is not given to you, do not on that account mistreat the one from whom you seek it, because he does his duty when he sets an end to his defense. Likewise, we say more to you, that you should wish to conceal and keep secret our testament from all those from whom

--- Page M117 (printed page 123, Liber Mercuriorum, Cap. XL cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

you seek the practice, because it is forbidden. Because if they wished beyond their ignorance to fail at showing it, they would beware for themselves through prohibition. However, and nevertheless, returning to our purpose through the said testament for reformation, I teach you that you should dissolve the terminated moisture of silver through reiteration of liquefaction, and at the bottom of the vessel there will remain burned and combusted a black earth with some small amount of the substance of the said quicksilver. Son, this earth is the belly of our sulfur, whose dissolution is made solemnly through resolution into its own quicksilver, from which it was complexioned, and therefore some of the philosophers said that sulfur can be dissolved only through the benefit of its own quicksilver, namely when it is congealed through its own vapor, then it is dissolved, and is transmuted into a clear, diaphanous nature, like a spirit.

--- Page M118 (printed page 124, Liber Mercuriorum, Cap. XL cont. / Cap. XLII) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

Wherefore it is evident that the body has returned to its first pure nature, and proximate to its own generation, namely into its own sulfur and quicksilver, which is like clear water: when therefore you have well whitened your body, sublimate it, and what you find ascended above its thick and white dregs, immediately imbibe with oil, and fix it, project one weight upon 50 of copper, and it will tint it into fine and true silver without any corruption. This sulfur, son, is immediately fixed and entering, on account of the dictate of nature, because it is akin and neighbor to the said copper, and the powders which you will find sublimated on the surface in your sublimatory, keep aside, because that sulfur is of another power, as you alone can prove for yourself through experiment.

CAPVT XLII.
On the work of lead.

Son, lead in its nature contains a great part of combustible fatness and of the defect which is in its substance: on account of this it is that its earthy part was not

--- Page M119 (printed page 125, Liber Mercuriorum, Cap. XLII cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

well mixed through dry water according to the reason of its gross substance, nor was it well purified: and therefore it is necessary for you to divide and separate that lead from its said substances, and that each of them be well purified by drawing out and putting forth the fat sliminess in the elementation, which occurs through condensation and rarefaction in moist matter. Son, when you wish to condense water or air, place fire in the earth, which causes a vacancy of the entity of earth, and when you wish to condense the air, draw it out, and rarefy the water through gentle softening, until you see your body lightened. Afterwards cook the body in water, and afterwards sublimate it, and when it has been sublimated, the body is called a wonderful and exalted salt, which we properly call the salt and

--- Page M120 (printed page 126, Liber Mercuriorum, Cap. XLII cont. / Cap. XLIII) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

sulfur of nature. Incerate this salt of nature with simple oil of silver, until it is fixed and flowing, and of that project one weight upon 50 of lead, and it will convert it into the purest silver. And if you wish to congeal and perfectly change quicksilver into fixed silver, mortify it through the vapor of its own body: afterwards make projection upon it, namely one weight upon 50 of the said sun, and it will fix it into true silver through its natural heat.

CAPVT XLIII.
On the work of tin.

In a similar manner you should do with tin, as you did with lead, which need not be separated except as when you are in the reduction; nevertheless you shall conjoin it gradually in gradual decoctions, because it holds little sulfurous virtue, except in the substance of its air. Therefore the said vir-

--- Page M119 (printed page 121, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLIIII) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

nevertheless suffused through decoctions, through the attenuation and subtilization of its substance, I shall make it so for you, which is a great reduction [?] to another thing. Therefore sublimate it as much as you wish after immersion, until you obtain from it a salt of nature, and with this sharp substance incerate it until it is fixed, and project one weight upon 100 [?] weights of tin, and it will transmute the substance into true silver, and if you apply the proportion of it upon congealed quicksilver, it will fix it into quicksilver in perfect retention.

CAPVT XLIIII.
On the congelation of quicksilver for reproducing medicines in metallic transmutation.

Take the earth of lead and all its four elements, and suspend as much quicksilver as your sense will demonstrate to you in proportion to the power

--- Page M120 (printed page 122, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLIIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

of the earth upon them in such a manner that it can receive the vapors of sulfur, and light a fire beneath, and receive the congealed and suspended substances for hours. But since this work, if it be done by sustained effort, is tedious, of little [?] value in comparison with other operations, when at your pleasure you can obtain so much from quicksilver by your own calling to do it, and by action, we advise you to proceed in your projection upon metallic bodies. But beware, because here we can [obtain] gold [?], the transmutation of both shall be, and you shall see and know our [teaching], whether of our essences of metals, just as our receipt and definition of its nature is. Do not understand, however, that in the projection of the superior medicine it suffices through congealed quicksilver, as it is necessary to receive noble branches having so much potency.

CAPVT XLV.
On the works of Venus and Mars.

If you wish to make preparations of Venus and Mars, you may think perceptibly of lead and tin, of those

--- Page M121 (printed page 123, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLV cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

of those, however, you should take nothing except according to the earth, and cook it in the water of whichever metal, until you have an honored [?] assaying substance. Incerate this with oil of R. And if the salt be of the nature of O, it is truly possible, otherwise it nearly suffices, and in gentle preparation it converts the other [?] into R better than from the mine. And if the salt be of nature, of O, project upon as much by weight likewise, and it will convert it into R. And if you wish to make Mercury, to facilitate the work of love [?], we shall teach you to lift it. And if you wish to purge upon Q, add salt of Q with salt of O through dissolution, so that you may extract the body gradually into its nature, which otherwise would not be anything [?], then dissolve it to remove [it], except through the bond of love [?] itself, let it be through the dissolution of R, and not with common water, but in Mercurial water sufficiently suffused, full, which the weight [?] may be directed, and through the way of dissolution of O or of R, understand this of all others. And if you well under-

RAYMVNDI LVLLI

understand this, you have the mastery of this entire science, through which the secret of nature is drawn from potency into action.

CAPVT XLV.
On body and spirit together, and concerning human nature.

When you wish to make something [?], as if sacred or of one body [?] through our mastery, you should first know how human nature exists through it, and according to this, as before, you should likewise have it in this work and operation. The property of human generation is, namely, this: The sperm is hot, glandular and fatty, and therein is the heat of the formative virtue in a simple salt, and in germinating matter, and the first thing is that which has to generate from within in you from itself. And in simple matter, it is cold and watery in the third point, in the half-point receptive in the nature of corruption,

--- Page M123 (printed page 125, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLV cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

and therefore when they are mixed together and congregated by the benefit of heat, of lascivious lust, it germinates corruption. When the heat of ardor, on account of the perforation [?] beyond the cold of the watery element in the spermatic matter of the woman, extends itself, otherwise it is extinguished: and thus by reason of the possession of the half-point of cold and as natural heat has returned into itself through all parts of the water, the spermatic particle in such a manner that the mixture is easily homogeneous in the form of clear water. Know then with what intention nature makes this mixture of simples, that is, and this homogeneous composition in the hidden virtues of the first complexion, from which the fetus was [or will be] complexioned in all its parts. And that each part may love the other in complexion, just as in the body by virtue and nature. This knowledge of nature has been useful, the other [being] undigested information, it suffices by such [means], through which the virtue of information has

--- Page M124 (printed page 126, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLV cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

the ability to position itself for forming the body through all the resolved parts of its mass, and they will be co-penetrated throughout its entire natural body. When the natural heat shall have been dulled in the moist matter by suffering on account of the half-point, and cannot have its sharpness of composition for penetrating to the performance of the work, it shall have itself and the moisture of the aforesaid mixture, and the heat resolves toward the subtracted moisture and the intention of the abstracted heat, and will resolve from the moisture of the aforesaid mixture, as much as that half-point permits, through which changes the heats shall be, and beyond the others it shall animate another half of this point, from its middle it gave to the spirit one point, so that you may say the heat has moderate sharpness for diverse figures, as may be suitable and adapted to the sacred [design] of our nature. And if this happens, nature [loses] its color, the extraneous humor is to be drawn out, which it held with mortification, which

--- Page M125 (printed page 127, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLV cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

which humor indeed, by nature's command, is transferred vaporizably into another region, in which it will be met by the menstrual moisture, and there it is fermented by the exuberant heat tempering the way for nourishing it together: by the virtue of this fermentation I wish our [principles] given over from the said spermatic [substances] to be resolved, and sublimated into milk, and in the mammary alembics it is informed and transformed. Wherefore, know [?] that as there are three voices, three receptacles, three milks, the moisture properly of spermatic substance enters, which we call and name the radical moisture. Wherefore we say it is, therein, insofar as the given [moisture] exists in milk, tasteless but sweet, signifying sweetness through the revocation of the humor in which the spermatic virtue remains: Therefore manifestly, the harmful [substance] was subtracted from your dear infant, so it is, and you shall distribute it until it generates teeth. And if it shall be at that mature age

--- Page M126 (printed page 128, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLV cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

in our mastery you should have this as a mistress and guide, the first [principle], since without it you can do nothing. Afterwards, from the thorny branches [?], take the body with the union and natural conjunction of quicksilver with it, and of quicksilver with the body, and with the weights of its virtue, make them embrace in such a manner that the virtue of nature has been conjoined as heretofore declared through allocations, and the multiplication of the body is co-contained in the parts of quicksilver, and the quicksilvers in the parts of the body, and when they are co-infused, let them be cooked somewhat, and from the whole let one complexioned white be made, whether of pure metallic [substance], from that which is already informed, observe the mixed [substance], lest by excessive heat and agitated motion, placing in more quicksilver than suffices according to the mutations of virtue, in such a manner that these two moistures may be mixed together: and make this through a wondrous glass, then you may place your [work] in a place of digestion, set in a pit, which al-

--- Page M127 (printed page 129, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLV cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

it is of the nature of the toad, until it has eaten and corroded all its bones, that is, its framework. Son, the bones of the toad are the covering [?], which are dissolved in the false water by virtue of the venom, when the fleeing dragon flies through the air, and when you see that all things have been resolved in the waters, the thorns well [cover] the surface of humidity, which holds the natural heat dulled from the dispersion of the sperm, then fermented and discharged, and until you see the said heat excited from sharpness and acuity, which now are the property of the nature of the true falsifier [?]. Therefore act cautiously, son, in that which without the guidance of caution and discretion in administering the fire gradually, and each time increasing according to the reason of nature, until you see the body in observation perfected: for be cautious lest evils come upon you in their coming. Son, unless you regulate yourself, the spirit will depart from the body, and you will be able to retain the soul

--- Page M128 (printed page 130, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLV cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

the soul it will not be able [to retain]. Therefore guard well that the body be not entirely deprived and expelled from this spirit through the pretense of exhalations, and at the minimum let it be externally [contained]. For the spirit is that which retains the soul, and the behaviors of the animal spirit within through itself, the spirit of the body holding [it] in the body, because the soul does not wish, nor does it seek to remain in the body, except through the mediation of the spirit. And when all three are united, each one would be separated from the others, because the spirit departing from the body, retaining the soul, sends the soul back to the body in a triangular form. We call spirit thus everything that remains in the body, and everything that is congregated in it; secondly, we call earth that which operates from itself through subtilization in the radical moisture of nature. And when you see the body thus dissolved, we call that body the hunted [body]. Give from its menstrual [substance], discharged, a portion of its own mixture

--- Page M129 (printed page 131, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVI) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

of its own proper nature, or from that which has been discharged let it receive beyond what it lost in its dissolution. Afterwards add [that] of its own proper nature, which must restore and fill it, and when it is fixed, let it flow, of which project one weight upon a thousand [parts] of quicksilver, or upon a thousand weights of any body, and it will convert that into silver or gold. Recognize, son, in the operation of the body, and in the reclamation perhaps by dissolving it, since water is the medium of separating the tinctures of all elements, and adding those to the royal crown through the triangular figure.

CAPVT XLVI.
On the conditions of sufficient medicines, what they are, and how they exist.

The first condition of the elixir is that it have the power of separating and segregating and removing every extraneous and unclean and corruptible superfluity from all infirm bodies in such a manner that everything leprous and corrupt in fu-

--- Page M130 (printed page 132, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVI cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

sion after projection may be separated, and thus I conclude in the matter of medicines and acuity, so that the disposition of the elements, according to how they were collected through the mixture of the humor of things altered by impregnated virtues, and mixed and terminated through the operations of the ministry of great sublimation [may be fulfilled]. The second condition is that the medicine have the power of illuminating and ennobling and altering every body into the most resplendent whiteness, or into citrine color according to the intention, which persons will place more, or may perfect a body diminished in perfection, and let it be complete from the whole into the whole. And the medicine acquires this condition through the virtue of the noble preparation of its matter from the [refined crucible] [?], through which the medicine may afterward be easily made in such a manner that the matter may be elementally dissolved, and afterward in the depth of the body may be mixed without separation,

--- Page M131 (printed page 133, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVI cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

but this cannot be done unless its bodily substance has been strongly and very well sublimated and subtilized through dissolutions and sublimations made through distinct operations, and thus of the parts, the falseness and splendor of their illuminations and nobility. The third condition of the medicine, which it would have through its principal virtue, is that it alter the metal either to the finding of its own praise, or into a determined solar fusion to the end, so that the metal thus exhibited [passes] all assays, and [is] susceptible to all proofs and tests, and this medicine cannot be placed [against] greater or stronger tests of assay: and the medicine acquires this virtue through the operation of industrious effort and sufficiency in the calx, until the uncertain becomes effective [?] and the arrival of the illustrious and fundamental [substance], as if all phlegmatic extraneous moisture, which previously could not be

--- Page M132 (printed page 134, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVI cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

endured by the virtue of the optic [?] fire, separating it, may be entirely dissipated, when the purity is allocated in the calx, thus hardened through the recreation of sublimation of the unfixed part upon the fixed matter and the fixed material. The fourth condition is that the medicine have perpetuity through a firm alteration of impression, wholly without separation, and without any flight or evacuation of its former [state]. And this condition comes from the virtue of its own proper fusion after the preparation of its clear and splendent subtlety with its stable ferment. The fifth condition is that the medicine have the virtue of giving and bestowing the weights of perfection with respect to the limits of weight of perfect metals: this condition comes to it through the property of the subtle medicine, and through the binding and congregation of an operation that has made the gathering of very many minimal parts

--- Page M133 (printed page 135, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVI cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

added and congregated into one. If you wish to know whether you have the altering [substances] and the altered: the things that alter are quicksilvers. The altered things are the bodies that have received fusion, since they are altered or first altered, and secondly they are alternated. And because in those altering [substances] themselves, and the greater part in them, there is a uniformity that is lacking [in] this or that, therefore naturally bodies are altered, and it speaks by reason of its uniformity, and by reason of its density it has one [measure] of good weight, and therefore by reason it is necessary for us to consider that which, after experience, has the value of clarity, to understand how from Mercury the true Medicine must be created more than from other spirits through the concordance of diffusion according to the state of its preparation. If you have, son, the memory of its intentions and as the greatest philosopher of the great royal philosophy, know that the modern philosophers and the young

--- Page M134 (printed page 136, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVI cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

in the knowledge of the virtue of nature are deficient, more in strengthening the natural virtue than in preparing the matter against corruption, and [in] other matters, because they do not know how to well compose their strengthening medicines, in which there is more of form than of matter, and they do not know how to corrupt the medicinal matter extracted from the corruption of its own proper nature, nor however the virtue infused by celestial influx and fixed in it through the course of nature with gentle decoction of the sun and the stars. Which necessary thing is, that the natural virtue, which is given from heaven, should thus be guarded [so as to preserve] the natural celestial virtue transmitted, treated by the virtue of the subtle medicine, and [freed] from its corruptions. By virtue of good understanding, which the good Physician discerns, through which the medicinal matter is treated and terminated under the conservation of the good virtue, through which it must oper-

--- Page M137 (printed page 141, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVI cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

operate. And therefore, when one is ignorant either through the deficiency of unknown evident extraction and composition of such medicines, or much deceived through formless future and renewed excitations. And, because we the experienced are ignorant of the measure of the strength of the bond by which the operative virtue is bound in its matter, it is necessary that the entities of color superabound beyond their degrees through the power of the virtue of the bond: and therefore the said virtue is dissipated and dissolved from its subject, if it flees from the burning and cremating fire, its spiritedness is mortal, and from that matter the confusion of your virtue arises. And the reason why the virtue withdraws from fire is this: because its subsection, or body, has not been exalted [by] the said virtue before the fire has been distant beyond the fortunate measure. Thus therefore its corroborative virtue is liquefied in its being, and it guards, and burns in common fire applied with natural fire, in which

--- Page M138 (printed page 142, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVI cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

in which is the celestial virtue that you seek. Afterward by such information it is informed that the common fire does not overcome the said medicinal preparation beyond the region of animation of those wishing to assist nature through strengthening, so that they may learn to attract known virtues through the preceding doctrine from vegetable, mineral, and animal bodies, or from onion-like [substances], others, [and] the stone of our laborers. And to this end we rouse your intellect in the water of simple composition. Son, it is necessary for you to apply the attracted virtues to the habitual magisteries, to disturb through their strengthening by the projection of matter: therefore when you wish to strengthen, apply [it] so that the virtue may be able to be habituated to it, and that it may be beyond the simple, and [available] to men for performing their actions. And if you set forth against [disease] you will be able to exhibit, and set forth against all other infirmities, because the natural heat expand-

--- Page M139 (printed page 143, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVI cont.) ---

expanding itself, and you will set forth externally the virtue of the strengthening medicine [which] will expel the causes, and break through those things that are curable, because what is contrary has joined enmity, for indeed what is homogeneous in its quiddity [the medicine] segregates and separates the heterogeneous, and let the homogeneous contraries be [united]. See for yourself, and if the matter is not subtle, in which you collect the essential virtues, let it not be of things that spring forth in threads [?], nor of water in its smallest and insensible parts and particles, so that the natural virtue may cause the patient to receive in great abundance such strengthening [as] to persist in the innumerable formal particulars of the queen [?], to heal, and without the injuries of other spirits, which through too much motion in such a cause must be exhibited in the making: and this is against the strengthening, in which a certain virtue is obstructed by our sensible moving bodies. And for accomplishing such a thing, medicines above all others among all medicinal, vegetable, and ani-

animal [substances], the bosom of gold has been designated for the confection of medicines. And if above all virtues among vegetables and animals their virtues are to be honored successively by right, and [from] the much-blessed [bee], which composes honey. You must season the matter with the said virtues, either in the substance of the composition, and in the combustions of the sides, or in the impregnation mixed through the union of small portions of their miscible substitutes without great vexation of those things appointed through fire, the quantity of which we shall speak of in chapters 8 and the confirmation: and such repetition of excitation, if there be understanding, you can make diverse medicines of other things, which will be of no cost for conforming to human nature, as we have declared to you in the practical medicinal treatise, and its annotations. And if you understand the masterwork in its depths, how they are, you will also understand all other medicines under the observation of the strengthening virtue.

CAPVT XLVII.
On the method of separating elements from one another, for composing brief operations through the letters A B C, and their composites, and the confection of things.

When you have made H, project that from B, afterward make the separation of H, and of B through I, and put H aside after sowing through I, and mix the substance of B of materials in the furnace, then put H in B, and put [it] nevertheless in V, and afterward separate H Q C through Z, and afterward separate H from C through E, and take from B that which H manifests to you through X, and you will have the first element, which will be the elemental wine. Note, and observe, because to have what is the second element, take C, and transfer that through X, and afterward add with it one [part and] put that in the chamber of V through V, and sublimate it, separating H C D through E, and take from B that which H manifests to you through X, and I, put the said H through C, and from that which remains of H by the benefit of X, and I, afterward the second element, which

--- Page M142 (printed page 146, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

we call mineral water. And when you wish to take what is the third element, be upon D above, and transfer that through X, and put and add with H, and enclose in the chamber of V for one natural day, afterward separate E with H through Z, and immediately separate H through V. And take [and] distill that which will remain from E, and you will have the third element, which is called your moist air. And if you wish to draw forth [fire], because sometimes it is taken in place of air, you shall place those when you transfer through X, and add with H, and when they have been in the chamber of V for 12 [days], afterward separate H through V, taking from E what will remain to it of H through the benefit of X. And in a similar manner do [this] if from L you will have fire in the hot nature of the catabolisms [?], and all four [elements] and nature [derive from] Mercury from living silver. And if you do not well understand, we tell you: and the sublimations, and know for yourself concerning those distillations.

--- Page M143 (printed page 147, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVII cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

And from all other bodies understand [likewise]: after this, being prepared to create a just fusion, which I wish as much from bodies as from Mercury, conjointly or separately, as you will find through the tables of metals, through which the mystical wheel concordantly [arranges] all things in every form of metals. And when you mix from the matrix of Venus and Mars, or from both together, or whether through the fusion or the expression of their elements, you shall place nothing but B. But stripped [matter] or what is of the middle [sort] is infirm, unless they are our minerals, in which our quicksilver congeals within [them], and the simples less than what we call the formative stone, and from that confection you will find particular works, son, which are within the breadth of the great magisterium, nor [need] we [do] anything but to run the elements of Mercury through all metallic elements, both simply

--- Page M144 (printed page 148, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

and in composition, as we have shown you, and [as is] said through the tables of this art, and as many and innumerable conjunctions as you can generate through fusion of diverse virtues, and from as many sulphurous [substances] as you will have, so many particular works you will have. And if from the sulphurous [substances] you make fitting [compounds], and from as many conjunctions as you make, so many works you will have of particular composition for projecting or making projection upon as many metals as their particular medicine will have been composed from. And by that method you [should] move yourself toward all metallic elements from all [forms of] Mercury through the first chambers to the end, which afterward may be led according to the nature of their elementary parts to form and to fixation, so that by fixation through the course of generated sulphurous [substances] of sufficient [quality] from those, or and their natures with the third letters, and the fourth [letters] of the Sun or of Mercury together

--- Page M147 (printed page 149, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVII cont.) ---

to red or of the Sun only, and to white of the Moon alone or of it and of Mercury, of which [they] consist and according to their medicines. And it sets forth to you through liquefaction and tables to show. For from what is drawn from H consists Ip F Mercurial still through G, and then applicable with K white. Likewise if you seek to add [a] Mercurial with Ip born, by reason of such it will be to be exhibited. Join therefore what you seek of those two I with K simply or in composition, and it will give you a wondrous and admirable fusion through the potion of nature. And as it is said of white, so it is said of red.

CAPVT XLVIII.
On the nature of the above-mentioned ferments.

Through the middle ferments one can ferment the natures of the whole body. And because we have not yet spoken of those, we say

--- Page M148 (printed page 150, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

to you that before you wish to ferment, see first that the ferments are well prepared. Concerning the prepared ferment, it is [necessary] that it first be calcined, then sublimated, and as we connect through three principals: by no means should you make fermentation from that. Nor is it needful for you that first a calcined powder be made through liquefaction. Second, a distilled powder through dissolution. Third, well inserted, [is] the powder of the body through congelation: and the sublimated powder through separation: clothing and softening it is our secret: because without those, no simple body can [avail] you in perfect union, unless it be altered with the greatest [degree of] alteration through vermilion [?], for the love of which, the others will not be fit for operating. The ferment is a perfect sublimated body, and the altered [substance] said [to be] the converting part and into a form of composition. Since no sublimated body is, with a stone of nature unknown [?], which draws to itself as the

--- Page M149 (printed page 151, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVIII cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

lodestone draws iron, and the elixir, which has relaxation to give in the aid of the moist elements, of which those that are subtle powders, which are and will be immediately sublimated upon the body's stability. Thus therefore if you intend to reiterate the filings of subtiliation of such a body in the mixture of sublimated moist spirit, however many times you perform this exercise, you will gain 10 parts in powder. So this [proceeds] so that the sublimated body and the volatile [substance] hold themselves together, and so much better for placing one [part] from V upon a hundred, and a hundred upon a thousand, and a thousand upon 10,000, and 10,000 upon 100,000. And so on to infinity. For it will bring the body to itself first to white, and if [there be] redness, to red: thus is the marriage of these, and of the stone, in what manner: when you have made the body fixed through the medium of water of Mercury, then give it fusion through subtle distillation, note, and if you wish, that they have a little of the natal [quality], and so if [you proceed by] the tone through the way of reduction, and if the tincture so superabounds

--- Page M150 (printed page 152, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLVIII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

[it will be] a tincture of incomparable price, and as has been said to you concerning the sublimation of the ferment, so certainly you must understand concerning the sublimation of the body in the stone through those elements. Furthermore we reveal to you, so that you may understand the notation that has been given to you, that the aforesaid stone comes from our quicksilver, which in its nature carries the virtue of the adamant, so of its bodies, salt of such kind, as if in its nature the first virtues of the stone are dissolved, which sweetens [them].

CAPVT XLIX.
On other revelations of the properties of natures and of the remedies of things.

Furthermore we tell you that the said natures are not sharp missals [?], which you cannot have, and when they transfer themselves from the first into the second part. Those from which the tinctures are received are not the matter of nature, and the tinctures are of the proper mate-

--- Page M151 (printed page 153, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLIX cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

rial of the essential nature of their own nature. Therefore nature knows not how to add nor to aggregate, except through its own nature, namely through its own matter, through the intrinsic pre-assumption deposited by the artificer from the virtue of the mercuries in the aid of the stone, namely of the metals and of the matter of elements. In the virtue of the vital [forces] called and named by it, [is] the vitreous earth created from quicksilver in the proposition of nature, whose material parts, those which are of the proper essence of the tincture, are our fires, which likewise we could not have, except [for] the wise, who are the seed of the first part, who first bestow the connections of nature in the said regions, so that you may have the animate essence of the tincture, and for making other principal medicines. Therefore, of sulphur and quicksilver, if it is in that nature. Three things there are of good appearance and they communicate, of which nature is impregnated. Because natu-

--- Page M152 (printed page 154, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLIX cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

the material is impregnated by the formal nature. And from that, nature [proceeds] to make its revolutions against extreme corruption, which it despaired of [as] its abjection. Therefore, if you have understood us, we shall tell you that never will our quicksilver be without sulphur, as it already is [known]. With it [sulphur] it is converted, nor can sulphur exist without its own nature, which is the principal medicine of attainments, through which all nature [operates] for making and for fulfilling its generations: unless nature, thus driven, has scarcely [anything] in its operations [if] the parts of its matter opposing cannot, nor if salt could not subject the parts of the moist, and unless it had sulphur, they could not cook, nor congeal the moist parts which are to be formed. And unless it had salt, the parts which are to be preserved could not penetrate on account of the scattered unification. And hence we call it magnificent salt, good

--- Page M153 (printed page 155, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLIX cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

honored salt, animated salt, because it is begotten of hot nature, which penetrates, binds, impregnates, and fills. And so by virtue of heat, and because the matter of nature excels, it penetrates and makes penetrable the goodness of the aggregation of appearance through the great capacity [?] in its radicality. And so our salt follows the formal harmonic mixture of all elements. Wherefore do not wonder that it is called harmonic salt. For unless it is called [so], beyond it [there is merely] the pure sublimation property of flint [?], and the first matter of nature, and all of nature in it, because it is of nature, just as the smith is in his forge, which if it should fail for operating, could inform another. And just as that fire of nature is, so nature in the philosophical art takes [it] as the natural heat existing as complexionate according to its own nature for the subject. And so all the good qualities are called salt in it, unit-

--- Page M154 (printed page 156, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLIX cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

ed, burning and ardent, and it is called the salt of metals, water of the star, sea star, stupefied [substance] flying, the fire of command [?]. Salt of the nature of health, salt of nourishment, borax, ash of borax, salt of urine, sal ammoniac, Attincar, salt of attincar, salt of soap, salt of alkali, Soda, congealed clouds, congealed vortex. The corporeal holder, secret of nature, frozen sea water. Sublimated arsenic, sulphur exalted in the heaven, exalted sulphur, and sublimated Mercury. And all these things cause the auxiliaries to err, who through their false ignorance and the error of certain [persons concerning] the matter of their reward and the choosing of its proper properties, whence they excuse [themselves], and being deceived by the art, they are as little children. But it is for you through revelation I say that well-remembered things be given and stored in memory, if in your mind, in your own sense of the proper part, because at no time should you understand here about other salts, unless it is about metals, which in

--- Page M155 (printed page 157, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLIX cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

they will be dissolved, just as through the art you are able to achieve the same. Therefore sweeten that salt; know that it will enter bodies as a true nature, which wishes to be within, and from it has [the power] to transform from one into another natural species, whether of metal and nearly so, of metal and of calamine fire. And therefore all salts are part of the nature of metals, of metallic property, on account of their affinity, and the adamant is congealed in furnaces, giving since it is nothing but [?] the virtue of the living, it is able to rise again into a precious stone, its matter and nature having been altered and changed to a noble [form]. For this perfect mixture has set forth the true discernment of nature, by which [things] wretched can be perfectly diminished, speaking corporeally. Know, O son, why it is such and is reputed to be of that [nature], for unless it were secret, they would not be composites. Also because you trust it to be formed, nor if the nature of its operation is to be concealed,

--- Page M156 (printed page 158, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLIX cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

its being is complete. And because nature revealed [and] cast out from its own house, like a man who is exiled from his possession, and as he would wish to come, just as things which are vile, and the coloring of its own body. Therefore it willingly seeks strongly and at the same time through snares, and suffers to deign to exercise its own [powers], or likewise to retain, in which things it is finished. And see to this, that it is found in every place, and without it no thing could be generated. It has deceived widely and broadly, in the forms of bridges, it is sold in labors and in them traitors do not come, and it receives in itself a jubilant termination through its own ultimate extremity, as if complete. Whence Virgil [?] composed these verses from [his] speeches.

Virtue indeed on the earth, or in its subdivision, rejects.
Four things inscribed, nature is referred to as evil,
But frost, air, earth, what water endures,
and falsely transgressions or life does not rise again.
What [?] is the grain but by many philosophers
the short [path] has been seen through the royal form.

Therefore certain [persons] were not seen [as] good, because they did not have license from the masters of nature, except metaphorically in the obscure coating of enigmatic words, as through their words consistently following.

Nor let a good state be avoided through the circumstance
Of vice; let punishment be salutary with virtue.

Son, he says, through the regime of fixation he says [concerning] the hardening of nature and forces, and if you feel that we say anything to you of a title of the law concerning nature, you should understand how Jesus Christ [wished] to restore us through nature, the false through transformation, whether through this, through accident to its being. We were all condemned. It does not appear that thus to flourish is of injustice moreover; this appeared to you, because of one virtue of goodness he wished to have foretold the nature of the triangle, through which he demonstrates, with lowly humility, patience, goodness, of magnitude, of eternity, of power, of wisdom, of love, of virtue, of truth, of glory, of perfection, of justice, of generosity, of mercy, and of dominion through various prefaces from the art of the strict [authorities] in this present testament we commend. And if you understand this radically, you will understand through nature the part of the trian-

--- Page M159 (printed page 161, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput L cont.) ---
