what form is of the virtues of the trinity and of the unity of all intellectual and sensual [things], the procurative [power] is extractive, and of every natural [thing], and of the intellection which are of the operation of divinity [?], and consequently all others, which are contained in the vows of T. And if the book says the same through the vows, for the vows constitute [?], and when through the works of the perditions you may understand these as of their signs, or their fascinations: whence I would wish to ask you in your [?] humility, that by this science I do not wish to harm you but in every increase [?] it can somehow be done [?], and according to what the Apostle says you ought, according to the ways you shall bind yourself, and, son, you ask that the will of the superiors [?] may penetrate by the force of contemplation [?] my own.
And then you will be able [to attain] to the vows [?] the vows of T, and which move through the vows and the living [force] of T, outside B red through X.
I ask, why do the virtues in the said [manner] make

--- Page M160 (printed page 162, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput L cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

a triangularity, or a divine triangle, and all others? I answer: this is because when the common virtue of the two natures of all is the mother. And the supreme essential virtues flow in, and they flow into the said material triangle, and through other natures constituting them in the quadragesimal [form], which are named the principal [virtues] of noble natures, namely among triangles and spheres. And you can understand what you are able, if you wish nature, you are able to form through the art or the diamond of virtues [?] the superior [nature], since the natural [thing] shows that from them you can form nothing. Wherefore, if fortune of nature follows [?], it is needful for you that you know the art of the chapter, or Virgil, when the good of our nature [was] true [?]. Nature cannot reform a thing into its first form, unless it has known [?] some aid of art. And this we have properly seen through

--- Page M161 (printed page 163, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput L cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

its science, when a body converted into a similar body, just as it was, unless it is turned: thus in powder it is in dry calcination, and in elixir in the moist [form], which converts all other into its own proper likeness. And this, though nature could do by license, it cannot [do] unless those knowing receive succession from the art, because it has joined of the kind [?] [that] art [is] form in the form of all bodies, wherefore it makes metal into powder, and from powder it makes elixir, and for all the infirm it truly commands an ointment of the righteous, if you wish: for thus the thing is, you should understand much, thus son, or [these are] the principles. Nature otherwise is lacking [?]. Nor indeed should the Volume [?] be said to be commanded for years in the morning; thus we approach having [?] 1000 and more than [?] twice learning: if you contend about silver. There is another body that it has transformed and changed, and [which] is adorned with virtues,

--- Page M162 (printed page 164, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput L cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

lead or tin, iron or copper, white or red, it is quicksilver, or whatever other metal it transfigures into the form and value of gold: concentrate, against that which resists, it shows, and better than the natural [substance] on account of those virtues, and it has more power against the test, and against the administration than the natural [substance]. Therefore we say all things, that what cements itself from gold, is made by art admitted or by nature: for it is better to burn with ardent fire with hostile eyes, than to experience the place of medicine through science [?]. And we call it purified [?] silver, impatiently full of rust [?] against nature, with which the Sophist operates, and which they know not how to channel, nor to command. Its preparation [is] in a clyster, nor after, to dissolve nor [?] itself through [?] in the same mode nature holds itself in its ablations. Such things, son, they thus believe entirely, when [?]

--- Page M161 (printed page 165, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLIX cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

they will divide their gold in substance and color, on account of the applications of extraneous powders, but it is greatly diminished from all its natural endowments, on account of the defect and regeneration of its purgations, because they do not know how to restore it, nor to complete the course of nature, through which all good gold acquires many great virtues and properties. Wherefore, son, we say to you, if you wish to complete the course of nature, so that your gold may have power over all natural gold, see to it that the stone of the first degree is well purged from all its corruption, and afterward restore to it the virtues which it lost, and which you will find in the spirits of the fifth essences. And the more virtues of those spirits you put in, the greater will be the infusion and multiplication of the medicine; and according to the virtues of the medicine, so will be the virtues of the reformed metals. And when you have placed the spirits

--- Page M162 (printed page 166, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLIX cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

in the stone, allow it to continue its motion until the virtues of the fifth essence resist the ardor and harshness of fire. Then, son, when you see such a sign, let it be a signification to you that the fifth motion of the higher nature has come without any corruption, from which comes the virtue of the fortification of nature. Son, just as nature in the digestion and preparation of its simple matter receives its fifth essential virtues from the specified influences produced from its own mineral, transmitted and remitted in place of the perfection of form by the precept of the Creator in very simple and most tenuous spirits, in respect of the fifth Number, and there they are terminated in rest. Similarly, after the resolution of the simple fifth spirits, and replete with celestial virtues, which descended there through the influence of the heavens into the said matters, we infuse those into the matter of the stone

--- Page M163 (printed page 167, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLIX cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

without injury to the virtues, with appropriate caution of fire according to the tolerance of the spirits in which they are retained, and those are terminated and retained both by the virtue of the stone and by the virtue of their own proper motion and subtle mixture, in as great a multiplication as we wish in number or quantity. But since there are few who understand or know how to perform this mastery, as has been said, therefore they believe and affirm that there is no other mastery than their own miracles of alchemy. Therefore, to avoid the evil which has always and often been delayed, we prohibit all common physicians from using their gold in alimentary medicines, that is, in foods and drinks, because immediately through the sharp things of fire contrary to human nature, which enter through its sophistication, they mortify the natural heat, and dissolve and resolve the

--- Page M164 (printed page 168, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput XLIX cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

spirits of the heart, in which the said natural heat is preserved. But let them have in their knowledge sound gold from its own proper mineral, or our gold of the mastery, examined through its own examinations in the proper tubes of cements. Son, the form which we told you of, the perfective form, infused into the matter of the purged stone in place of the action of every former, because formation is nothing other than the pure action of that skilled [agent] which is the principal perfective [cause], has given to it in potency, and on account of it the virtues will result which we told you of above. And if through this we define formation according to general philosophy, saying that formation is that thing to which it properly belongs to form, whence we say to you that you will find its action through the infused virtues in the spirits of the fifth essences, with the decoctions of gentle fires, in respect of celestial heat.

MERCVRIORVM LIB.
CAPVT L.
How errors and deposits should be corrected; and concerning impossibility.

THE impossibility is, son, that after some remaining change of matter [?] from color into color, to which by solicitation you should attend in such a manner that all colors be yours except redness, which matter comes after the separations of the fifth substances; then [there is] a simulation against the dry matter. Then, son, the fifth substances which remain in the dry [matter] are burned and ignited, and likewise their tincture, because they do not wish to be except as simple moist air, nor can they be drawn out unless they are artificially added to them, and by their attractive virtue are collected there through the air of their waters, and by revivification, as appears from the point of the last separation of the waters extracted in the bath. Unless you know how to extract air from the waters,

--- Page M166 (printed page 170, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput L cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

ask nature that it may wish to show you its [?]additive [power], how a small amount of it, by its [?]combustion, will separate the spirits, by which [?] ...the true attractive virtue exists, and is entirely separated. And beware of a great or excessive fire in the distillation of airs, because the body would become red, and the attractive virtue which had been given to you would be suffocated, and if it were at the degree of suffocation, it could draw more than you would need, because you have need of nothing but very subtle air. Therefore draw out through inhumations, since these guard the tinctures from all burning, and restore the lost moisture, revivifying the attractive virtue. And if you see that the body has become reddened, and its leaves are hot and scorching, so that they are often changed into the likeness of a burning coal, then cast it into its water

--- Page M167 (printed page 171, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput L cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

immediately, and in it cause it to be cooked until it begins to take on [?] a dark or white [appearance]; and extract all the sharp and acrid [parts] through [?] sweet [?] fire. The principal [?] colors are to be extracted, which give knowledge of that nature, and they are: blackness, whiteness, and redness, as we told you in the chapter, Always, son.

But here we tell you with the intention of extracting airs, and correcting errors, and guarding oneself from those errors: howsoever it may be, beware of all whitenesses and of many blacknesses, so that you have only one redness, which must come at the end of the first degree. And if it comes before, it signifies the combustibility of colors which wish to become red on account of the sharpness which they feel from the great inclination of the fire administered to them by bad management through stimulations, before

--- Page M168 (printed page 172, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput L cont. / Caput LI) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

their own proper motion is completed. And when they become red before [being ready], through weakness they revert from their own tincture by improper reddening. Because their reddening, gathered and collected, is in an extraneous matter which cannot endure fire: that is, when true preparation has not been given to it. And if you understand this well, you will know why the sophisticators fail to make true gold.

CAPVT LI.
De compositione aceti nostri, quod constringit argentum viuum ad extra ponendum naturam.

TAKE the ninth wine, son, well dried, and in it dissolve D to a certain part of the said wine, and as much of C. And if in place of the said wine you put the juice or mare of lunaria, one part will be worth ten. Afterward cause it to be distilled, and through the distillation the water

--- Page M169 (printed page 173, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput LI cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

receive, which is the juice of the exuberance of the monad, and look to why toward the sublimation of its Mercury, just as we told you before in the Book of Mercuries, stir up the golden clamor of that pore, as we told you at the beginning of our practice: and in it you may dissolve that which will be dissolvable from stones and metals. And if you make projection, you will find each of those things made to be attenuated, and the mineral virtue will be multiplied: and let this be revealed to you, that the vinification of infused virtues resides in materials with spiritual living properties in the said powders, which have been given doctrinally for gilding and for silvering, unless generally and by other sciences he be named otherwise, neither openly nor secretly, because this method is bestowed by hand for natural things by natural [means] and artifice, unless [it be] the proper motion of nature, through which blessed will be he to whom God our

--- Page M170 (printed page 174, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput LI cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

Lord shall wish to inspire the gifts of His grace: thus the Lord is the master of the art, who illuminates the heart of His [chosen ones], by whatever forms and ways He shall wish, since there are many who, through all that each similar one shall have known, err in their works and their premised suppositions, confounding everything [to] nakedness, except through a higher reformation. Wherefore let each one [see] why it is done artificially, and so let him fashion himself toward it. Let it be fulfilled through art and nature in A, and [let him see] that the Artisan and good operator of the art can ascend to high things by that means. And what we could learn was situated in the curative remedies of our terms against the combats P N A B Y T Z E A V I G Z E: all these letters with the others spoken of above, as they are prescribed, will be found as attestations in the compendium of the declaration of our art, V ule meccin, in the number of the philosophers with all the integrity and perfection of this testament.

CAPVT

--- Page M171 (printed page 175, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput LII) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.
CAPVT LII.
On projections or the method of medicines, both simple and by conversion.

PROJECTIONS are made upon fused bodies according to the greater and lesser virtues which the stone finds through its subtlety. Therefore, the more subtle the stone, the more penetrating it will be, and the more of bodies and the greater or lesser [need] of medicine. But there are many who, in the projections of the said medicines, through brief and particular courses, through evaporation have lost their forms and volatile powders, through alteration without the value of medicine they have been able [to do nothing]: for when one makes projection of it without [proper preparation], namely of the medical medicine upon too much body, it is of no effect in itself, neither in alteration. And if the said medicine has been made to be of no virtue, do not care to pour it out further: because if the fixation [upon] the metal in which the medicine was fused

--- Page M172 (printed page 176, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput LII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

is done through conversion, and they cause it to flow through cupellation, that which has been transmuted radically from the body into pure metals will be [subject] to all separation according to the greater and lesser [degree] to which that feeble spirit may have thickened, because previously it was modified in virtue, and the proportion itself changes all bodies, just as [they are] cured and purged from all leprosy, and [it is] the antidotary of every infirmity. And this [is due to] the natural heat of the substance of all natural bodies. The decoction insensibly draws all bodies into the deepest [part] of itself, strangles [them], whether by nature and its nature, and the radical moisture, which is the medium between them, through that said conversion of subversion, under the individual metallic conservation. This [is drawn] from the cooperating appetitive virtue of the impure and leprous metal, which draws from itself the spirit of the

--- Page M173 (printed page 177, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput LII cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

world, glorified, just as a thirsting and thirsty body drinks and imbibes, and draws even the cooperating [virtue] to the depths of its heart, and give the implicated aids by the virtue from the heat of moving nature, which fills and impels the formations of the spirit in the body, which is reformed when the parts, as if [suitable], come together and are mixed, and to such a degree the fixed parts by the virtue of the mixed spirit are coerced into their depths and retain themselves in perpetual life. And the fallen body of the metals [receives] the state of projection. Similarly, you can do this with Mercury: if the medicines from which you wish to make projection upon Mercury are not of subsisting substance, by reason of the hardness of their confection, the living entering [ingredient] would have the thickest quality, unless you congeal the Mercury by the virtue of another Mercury, not [by] the vapor of vegetable [matter], or of minerals transmuted into the fused [state], the congelation may be made, or you produce

--- Page M174 (printed page 178, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput LII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

it in such a vessel with a long neck, in which there be a multiplication of sulfur: and thus we have said in its congelation to designate it for projection. But if you wish to make projection of the superior medicine, do not care to congeal Mercury otherwise. For before [they reach] the pigment and [?] distilled elixir, they strike with their subtlety, because the thing is filled with all virtues. If, however, you are [dealing] with particular medicines of only such subtlety, where this is to be done, you will suffer the subtlety through solution and congelation, through the exaltation of the ferment, of which, if similarly upon one [part] of many parts one part enters, you will convert all of them. But we leave the certainty of weight in the projection to give the medicine itself [?] to the coming cure of that which we shall tell you, of no investigation to anyone, since it has been fully treated in our Council, in the projection of medicinal parts.

--- Page M175 (printed page 179, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput LII cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

Son, you will find your metal slightly altered to an extrinsic vileness, as much as it will have transmitted through conversion, whose fluxes it will not have wished, truly, when I demonstrate with fungibility and the adornment of its lucidity, or manifest clarity, as much as it is well purged through conversion: and similarly concerning lead, and make certain proportions of it to be struck, and consolidate one, and solidify another. And when through conversion it will have transmitted, you can see in yours that which has been transmuted from it into purified silver. And if those hardnesses come from the miniature to be virtuously weighed, with the greater and lesser [degree] of their preparations and sublimations, whatever mutative color of right, which have not only been given to the virtuous, whether from their degree of subtlety the depuration [comes], which by its proportion could well segregate, I separate the diverse heterogeneous

--- Page M176 (printed page 180, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput LII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

[matter which] is transformed, unless it be through the admixture of conversion, and the placement of the gross medicinal substance, in which the highest transmuting virtue has been collected, which impedes the spirit from fulfilling the complete citrine [color], which [virtue] having been released in that which is not of the essence of nature, and of whatever [thing], all that which is vicious and proper to nature to act as one [force], so that it can [transform] the metallic [substance] found into silver better than from the mine. And therefore in this case we assist the spirit through the deauration of that which is extrinsic, just as [we assist] nature. But entirely you restore, because you must place lead in the conversions, so that the medicine may produce the complete aid of citreous fire. When such medicines are made upon purified bodies, the stone and other bodies can then be [?] fashioned in kind. But if fused [substances] are given without ignition, the determined [ones]. And this, because nature suffers the contrary of formation.

--- Page M179 (printed page 181, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput LII cont.) ---
MERCVRIORVM LIB.

contrariety of the undigested material, which causes it to flow before the [proper] time, until through working it has been consumed, and the undigested material has been dissolved, which is the philosophers' stone dissolved and digested, [now] suitable. Therefore apply yourself manfully to your medicine through working, and in it you will find gold and silver, according to which the ratio of medicine is to those things [as] red [is to them]. When you wish to dissolve salt by liquefaction, you must fortify yourself with H B through V and through X, until it is dissolved. And if you wish to coagulate by deposition, you need C D F Y K, and if you wish to coagulate, sometimes the objection of subtle [matters] could reach into the letters of R [at] the entrance of this enclosure and the criteria, and the defenses are of the nature of K. And through the rest of all the flours [are confirmed] by attestations, through which the complement is the third potency: whence two generative [powers], prudence and charity suffice to receive their

--- Page M180 (printed page 182, Liber Mercuriorum, Caput LII cont.) ---
RAYMVNDI LVLLI

proper complement in the land of labor. Unless you understand us, repeat by reading each lesson again, if already in diverse readings you are doubting, or if in diverse chapters. And repeat each chapter. And within the chapter, divide. And each chapter will be for you one lesson, and [a thing] of recordable memory, and of intelligible understanding through an armed will, you will mix one lesson with another, because the understanding of one will open for you the understanding of another. And one thing extended and understood will make you understand other things through persistent adherence. And thus plainly, governed by calculation, it will not deprive you of its fruit.
